From the first /cp Short on, releases are 23:30 IST (11:30 PM) on YouTube (user correction 2 Oct 2026: brain freeze went out at 11:30 AM by mistake) (Data API) and 18:30 IST on Instagram (scheduled in Business Suite through Chrome, because the user's Facebook account is blocked and no Meta API app can exist). Add a row
here for every new Short, with its links.

## Lessons

**bent-wrist-fist** (29 Oct 2026, the coordinator's Short #8 of the day ("do as many videos you can"), 17:48-18:58 IST; a TEST Short,
the no-pill arm (11:30 slot); the first line of the Queue, promised by thumb-finger's caption and Next-up; short arm, 71 words -> 31.40 s;
hook formula The Direct Address as the test: "Bend your wrist all the way down." / "Now squeeze a tight fist." (after The Direct Address,
The Impossible Claim, The Impossible Claim); answer, a true claim and a metaphor, "Can't? Your fingers have no muscles. They're... puppets."
at 4.75 s; button "Now bend it down again... and watch the puppet flop." into the hook; caption question "Could you make a fist? Yes or
no?"; YouTube 7ZRNtkXNeS4, playlist Why Does Your Body Do That?, thumbnail + captions + playlist by the API, cover.jpg also into Studio's
slot (the grey processing placeholder); Instagram (Business Suite, Instagram only, cover set) + the Page (fb.mjs 1739343171533074) 7 Nov
18:30 IST. Three QA rounds, qa.py 18/18 in rounds 2 and 3; the final MP4 is build.sh's into a fresh work folder (mix.wav and timeline.json
byte-identical).)
World: mosquito-bites' `camp.js` (the campsite at night, `CAMPPAL` sleeves pushed up) and one-finger's `fist3d.js` (the 3D hand), plus a
new `bwhand.js` (borrow it): a wrist that bends (`bwRot`: the hand frame turns about its x axis through the wrist; `bwProj(V, th)` gives
`pa` for arm-frame points and `ph` for hand-frame points), the forearm as an oval tube (`bwArmRing`, a hull of rings with the flexor bulge
on the palm side, `bulge`) and the wrist joint as a hull of the end rings and a ball (`bwJointOutline`), both as depth-sorted items for
fkDraw (`bwArmItems`, `bwDraw`); the finger-closing strings from a puppet's control bar in the forearm over the wrist to each fingertip
(`bwStrings`: `slack` waves, `glow`, beads that ride with the bar), marionette strings from the fingertips up out of the frame (`bwTies`,
`jig`), the puppeteer (`bwPuppeteer`: the forearm muscle as a little character anchored in the forearm, holding the bar (`bar` callback
between his body and his arms), moods calm / happy / strain / worry / pant / shock / proud / sleep, `wave`, `lift`, a nightcap `cap`),
`bwBar`, `bwGlass` (hand + forearm as rosy glass), `bwStickItems` (a marshmallow stick across the palm, in pieces so the fingers wrap
round it; `drop` falls in screen space). Its scenes.js: ONE state for the whole Short (`bwS(t)`, valid at negative time for the loop;
every SC.<id> draws from it, so the hand never jumps at a cut), `bwCam` / `bwAng` (the 2D camera with rotation, the hand's yaw and pitch),
`bwView` (the 3D camera tied to the 2D one: see below), `bwHero` (campChar, the rig's arm on the screen's right hidden), `bwUpperArm`
(the sleeve from the rig's shoulder to the projected elbow), `bwTag`, `bwBurst`, `bwShake`, `SC.loop`, `SC.cover`.
- **A 3D forearm that stays on a 2D hero:** put the wrist and the elbow in WORLD coordinates (`BW_WR`, `BW_EL`), take the 3D camera's
  target screen point from the 2D camera (`toScreen(cam, wrist)`), its scale from the zoom (`f = px_per_cm * D * zoom / 2.9`) and its
  roll from the wrist-to-elbow direction on screen, and draw the upper arm from the rig's shoulder to the projected elbow. Every 2D camera
  move (zoom, pan, a Dutch tilt for the glass shots) then carries the hand with him.
- **The rig's 'L' and 'R' are SCREEN sides** (`hideArm: 'R'` hides the arm on the right of the frame: his anatomical left).
- **"See" was aligned to its "s" only:** qa.py's voice check failed it at -14.6 dB in the speech band (the hiss is above 4 kHz, the vowel
  0.1 s later); make_timeline.py now moves it onto the first speech-band burst (`T.bursts`). Read qc_inband's "short window" flags on
  sibilant words as this, not as a quiet take.
- **"Puppets" reads as strings that rise from the fingertips** out of the frame (a marionette); the same strings drawn along the fingers
  read as glowing fingers. A puppeteer that moves with his bar reads as sliding; anchored, with the bar coming down to his chest, he hauls.
- **A cartoon head is wide:** at zoom 3.2 the hiker's head was ~460 px across, so a face plus a hand beside it did not fit the key zone;
  zoom 3.0 and the wrist closer to the face fixed both edges.
- Voice: 419 characters, one take per block, no retakes; quota.mjs showed 5,014 before (it lags; about 3,400 really left after).
  Renders about 215 s for 942 frames (three full renders: two rounds and the build).

`sbSplash`, surface pops; the hero soaking `sbHero` (BARE palette, arms by elbow + wrist, `held` drawn in his layer with the hands again
over it); Saturn afloat `sbSatFloat` (the rings on the water, the globe 0.255 R under, the water's patch over its submerged part, a
meniscus, a pop-up offset `dy`), the see-through globe `sbSatGlass` (a scan wipe, gas bands, fizz, a tiny core), measuring cups `sbCup`
(water / a heaped swirl of Saturn), a scrub brush `sbBrush`; THE SPLIT-LEVEL SET `TK`: the tub cut open at the waterline (far wall, floor,
water tint, soft shafts, caustics, a silver surface, the cut near wall with claw feet `sbTankCut`), bubbles rising `sbBubbles`). Its
scenes.js: `hookA`/`hookSat`/`hookCam` valid at negative time (the loop shot lifts a fresh Saturn into his hands), the human balance in
`SC.cup`, the knock with the fist drawn again in front of the planet in `SC.why`, `tankScene` + `tankPlanets`/`planetY` (each planet's
drop, splash and landing from cues; heavy ones sink fast, Jupiter and Uranus slowly), the foam mound that buries him and pops from the
top `foamMound`/`foamPops`, `ringsDuck` (the duck paddles into the abandoned rings), `SC.cover` (SINK / OR FLOAT?).
- **The shape map counts an image as solid only when most of it is over 90 % opaque** (shapemap.js `idSolid`: alpha > 230 on at least 60 %
  of its visible pixels, judged on a 24 px copy). Saturn's first rings (C ring 0.42, A ring 0.88 opacity) failed it, so the light stage
  went on lighting his chest UNDER them: his shoulder's outline and neck shadow showed through the rings. Cartoon rings at 0.94-0.98
  opacity pass, and hide him.
- **Water in front of him is an image** (as sharks-older-than-trees found): a fill inside a clip left his body below the waterline in the
  shape map, and the light stage drew its rim and shading through the water. The front water is painted into its own canvas each frame
  and drawn with `drawImage` (`SB_WCAN`).
- **A ball held in two hands: put the wrists BEHIND it.** Hands drawn over the globe read as two blobs on its face (ears or eyes); hidden
  behind it, the forearms leading in read as holding.
- **A prop that floats nearer the camera than he sits hides his hand:** the knock missed in round 1 (his fist went past Saturn to the rim);
  the fist and forearm are drawn again after the planet, and fluff closes over the fist as it sinks in.
- **Check the Sun's screen position in every camera:** in the knock close-up it sat behind his head and its bloom washed his forehead;
  `bg: {sun: false}` for that shot.
- **The splash after the word:** he lets go during "Sink..." and it hits the water in the pause after it (SPLASH at -17 dB in its gap);
  Jupiter's splash the same, after "Jupiter?". What lands on a word is only low or only top (the pop-up on FLOATS, the balance's boing).
- **Earth is a marble next to Saturn** (true widths, `SB_SIZE`): a white outline keeps a small planet readable on blue water.
- Voice: 388 characters for the takes + 69 for a retake of the catch block + 138 for two seeds of a reworded one (the livelier, 10.9
  semitones, installed by hand) = 595. Render about 230 s for 1,021 frames (three renders: two rounds and the rebuild).

of drops); `LMPAL` a sky-blue linen shirt), `lm_brain.js` (time-flies' brain as `lmBrain` with a bib, moods chomp (jaws closing by

SPIT button), `lm_lab.js` (a salivation lab plate (clock, a SALIVA STUDY poster, a window, shelves), a bench, volunteers `lmVol` (curly,
bun, bald; cotton rolls in puffed cheeks, eyes on the TV, drool that runs off the chin, happy "aww" eyes), beakers `lmCup` with a level and a

Borrowed unchanged: tongue-twister's `tw_mouth.js` and `tw_face.js`. Its scenes.js: `lmHero` (the wedge between his mouth and his hand,
`lmHandWedge`; a pinch grip `lmGrip`; sour mouths `lmMouth` pucker/water; drool), `lmHeroPt` (a head or wrist point from the pose with no
drawing: a deterministic window spot), `hookA`/`hookCam` valid at negative time, the forehead window into the glass head (`secWindow`),
`insideState`, `labDraw`, the NICE rosette slapped on the lens (`rosette`), `SC.loop`, `SC.cover`.
- **A test that happens in the viewer's head is safest watched AND imagined:** watching someone bite a lemon makes saliva flow (Hagenmuller
  2014, Wang 2017) and imagining the taste of a lemon in view adds to it (Keesman 2016), but looking alone (Kerr 1961, Shannon 1974) and
  imagery alone (Drummond 1995, 24 people) did not reach significance. So the hook says "imagine" while the picture bites.
- **A prop he holds after the test must clear the caption line (y 1290-1370):** the wedge held at his chin sat under "THERE'S NO LEMON",
  so the ghost lemon and its pop were hidden; held up beside his jaw they read.
- **A glow drawn from screen coordinates needs `screenSpace()` first:** the ghost's `softDot(gctx, ...)` after the scene (whose last call
  is `applyCam(cam)`) landed in the sky above his head.
- **A label tied to a moving camera can show up early in the wrong place:** SPIT TAPS popped beside the brain while the camera was still
  travelling down to the taps; start it once the camera has arrived.
- **The effects bus is peak-normalised:** the gulp 5 dB down moved 2.6 dB in the mix; `levels={"sfx": -7}` brought every gag to -16..-22 dB
  in its gap against a -16 dB voice.
- **The answer's music:** a music box under "your brain just took a PRACTICE bite" put four words under 6 dB; a low pad under 420 Hz and one
  note in the gap before the line kept the line clear.
- Voice: 441 characters for the takes, 97 for auditions of two flat-sounding lines (none better: 1.6-2.8 st against the takes' 2.4-3.3).
  Render about 220 s for 994 frames.

World: four new files (borrow them): `tw_stage.js` (TWISTER NIGHT: a plum brick wall plate with curtains and a valance, a TONGUE / TWISTERS
neon that flickers, a string of bulbs that dips clear of his head, out-of-focus bokeh placed round his head, a spotlight cone and pool,
wedge monitors; `twCrowd` (heads from behind, phone lights, a cheer), `twMic` (a hand mic at a rig wrist), `twScore` (a SAY IT 5X FAST
board, unused), `TWPAL` (a teal bowling shirt)), `tw_face.js` (his talking mouth drawn over the rig's in head units: `twVisAt(tt, rate)`
the twister as 18 mouth shapes on his own clock, valid at negative time; `twMouth` P (lips pressed, cheeks puffed) / A I O U D K / grin /
smirk / ooh / gape; `twKnot` (his tongue out, tied in an overhand knot); `twJam` (P and K squeezing out of his lips together, then
popping); `twPuff`; `twHead`; `twCountHand` (a fist with 1-5 fingers up)), `tw_mouth.js` (after tongue-nose's side.js: his head from the
side as glass with a mouth that articulates: `twSection(t, {lips, back, tip, open, glowP/K/D, puffP/K})` -- lips that shut (P), the
back of the tongue up against the soft palate (K), the tip up behind the teeth (D), air that leaves from the lips or rides forward from
the back), `tw_lab.js` (a 2013 speech-lab booth: foam wall plate, a SPEECH LAB sign, a taped note, a studio mic on a boom with a pop
filter, headphones in head units, a monitor whose voice trace goes flat from the right with a WORDS/MIN readout). Its scenes.js:
`twStageHero` (mic hand, counting hand, a two-hand grip, talk marks off his lips), `hookA`/`hookCam` valid at negative time, the window on
his cheek into the side view (`secWindow`), `secState` (held closures over "P... pops" and "K,", quick ones when it speeds up),
`twSeq` (a P row and a K row of beats; on "easier rhythm" the K dots slide under the P dots), `twBubble` (five numbered tries, worse
each time; the fifth's letters fall), `SC.cover`.
- **A test the viewer SAYS:** the narrator says the phrase once from frame 1 (tempo 1.08), and the hero rattles it on his own faster
  clock (4.5 words a second, 3 mouth shapes a word), so his mouth never lip-syncs her voice; "Your turn" then hands it to the viewer.
  The trip cannot land by 3 s (it needs a try or two): the hero's knot lands on "fast" and the answer at 4.9 s says what happened.
- **The articulators as drums:** the slow P and K pops land in the gaps AFTER their words (the closure held over "P... pops", the burst
  after it), so their sounds play whole; when it speeds up, the pops run under the words as their edges only (a low knock for P, a
  high tick for K) and become the rhythm track.
- **The science:** most tongue-twister slips are not swaps but both sounds at once (Goldstein, Pouplier et al. 2007, Cognition: intrusions
  28.2 % of tokens, substitutions 4.3 %; faster and more repetitions = more); "pad kid poured curd pulled cod" made some MIT volunteers stop
  talking altogether (ASA press release via ScienceDaily, 5 Dec 2013; HuffPost misprints it "cold").
- **A flat take shows in numbers:** the payoff's p10-p90 pitch range was 4.3 semitones and its words 10 dB under the in-band median; one
  `--redo` (61 characters) gave 9.0 st. Back the old take up first (src/voice/<id>.*) so the better one can be kept.
- **A tick that marks the first try must outlive the second:** try 2 replaced try 1 at the instant the tick was due, so it never showed
  (found on round 2's sheet); the first try now stays up through its word.
- **A fact on a sign must clear the app's top band at every camera key:** the lab's MIT · 2013 note was fine at the wide key and under
  the band (y < 375) once the camera pushed in; the set was moved under it.
- Voice: 400 characters for the takes, 61 for the payoff retake. Render about 215 s for 1,012 frames (three renders: two rounds and the
  rebuild).

**sun-8-minutes** (29 Oct 2026, the coordinator's Short #2 of the day ("do as many videos you can"), 10:04-11:26 IST; THE EIGHTH
CURIOUS SHORT of the topic test, the no-pill arm (11:30 slot); short arm, 72 words -> 33.47 s; hook formula The Question, the exact

World: four new files (borrow them): `sv_beach.js` (a beach at noon with the horizon at his eye level: sky, a Sun high on the right
you can hide (`o.sun`), gulls, cumulus, a headland with a lighthouse (it blinks at night), the sea band with swell lines and the
Sun's glitter, a wash that breathes up wet sand with foam, dry sand with speckles, shells and footprints, a striped umbrella (a

all of it again at night with one switch `N` (navy sky, stars and the Milky Way, black sea)), `sv_hero.js` (the hiker in a coral
beach shirt and teal shorts, bare legs drawn in his layer's `pre`, sitting in the chair; a tall lemonade with a straw that reaches his
lips (`sip`), ice that clinks, a level that stays level; sunglasses that push up onto his forehead (`shadesUp`) or slide down his nose
for a squint over the frames (`shadesDown`); his free hand behind his head (`relax`, through a raised-elbow key) or up at the
shades (`liftL`); faces sip/aah/smug/deadpan/wide/read/squint/up; `svPortrait`), `sv_space.js` (a vertically tiling star field, the
Sun with granulation and prominences, a wall light switch floating in space (`svSwitch`), the POP, a chalk outline with SUN WAS
HERE (`svOutline`), Earth painted once from 3D noise (oceans, land, ice, clouds) with a day side, a night side and a separate city
lights layer (`svEarth({sun, lit})`: lit 0 = a dark navy ball with a blue rim and glowing cities), the road of sunshine
(`svRoad`: a lit highway with envelopes riding at the speed of light, dark behind its last envelope, LAST flag), a green road
sign), `sv_props.js` (an envelope with a wax seal and a light trail, a postmark SENT 8 MIN AGO, the SUNSHINE LEFT countdown, a
round window on space with a label, pill tags, a tumbleweed). Its scenes.js: `svLeft(t)` (8:20 held while she says "EIGHT
minutes", then running to 0:00 on "out" with the last three seconds slowed), `svNews(t)` (how far the dark has come: the same
clock drives the road's dark end, the orbit's ring and the countdown), `svRoadCam` (stop on the outline, travel to the road's
dark end and ride with it, dive to Earth), the orbit from above (`svOrbitScene`, `svRope`: the PULL rope frays where the news has
passed, so Earth circles a rope tied to nothing), `SC.cover`.
- **A one-breath claim hook can run long:** "If the Sun vanished right now, you'd keep sunbathing for EIGHT minutes." took 6.0 s on
  two seeds (the answer could not start by 5 s). Splitting it into the title's question ("What if the Sun vanished right now?",
  2.6 s) and the claim as the answer block (from 2.97 s) fixed the timing and made the hook the title word for word. qa.py's
  "you" list does not count "you'd" (a warning only).
- **A horizon at eye level makes a close-up hold sky:** with the sea's horizon at his eye height (world y 1418), the close-up at
  zoom 2.9 still shows sky, the Sun and the umbrella above the chair; a horizon far above him leaves only sea behind his head.
- **The Sun in the frame needs a modest glow:** R x 7 soft dots and sunRays 0.9 washed half the frame white; R x 4 / 1.8, a glow
  layer dot of R x 1.9 and rays 0.32 keep a bright Sun with a readable sky.
- **One clock for every picture of the same event** (`svNews`): the road's dark end, the orbit's ring and the countdown all
  read it, so the shots agree with each other and with "lights out".
- **A camera locked on a moving thing makes it look still:** the fly shot first followed Earth exactly and it sat in one spot for
  3 s; following at a lag (it slides across the frame) reads as speed.
- **Studio's subtitle editor from the Details page:** after Upload file > With timing > Continue (the armed SRT), its Done did not
  publish (a re-opened editor offered Publish); Languages > Edit subtitles > Publish gave "English subtitles published".
- The YouTube upload itself (1,600 units) went through the API at 11:13 IST with the quota "out" since 09:48; captions.insert (400)
  was refused again. Voice: 452 characters for the takes used, 243 more for auditions (hook2 and the button; the first takes were
  kept). Render about 225-260 s for 1,004 frames.

World: new `fog.js` (the lilac chaser, the fog, the hiker close under it), `seers.js` (his eye, front up, with a team of colour-seers),
`paint.js` (the brain's roller over the WHAT YOU SEE screen), `lab.js` (1950s chin rest, a suction cup with a cat picture on a stalk,

- **A test that IS a picture is drawn after the light and the lens** (`post.overlay`): the lilac chaser's grey (196,196,196) and pink
  are then exact on every frame. The fog round the hero uses a light with `pool: 0, halo: 0, contrast: 0, split: 0, fringe: 0`, and the
  overlay's field fades out over 120 px above his head with the vignette drawn into it, so the two meet with no seam (rows measured
  178-197, R = G = B). Bach's stimulus in proportion: 12 dots, dot radius 0.18 of the ring's, a solid core to 0.3 of the dot, magenta
  at about 0.6 alpha, one gap stepping every 4 frames (a lap in 1.6 s). The gap's clock runs back from the end in the button shot so
  the loop goes round without a jump; the score's music box (four notes a lap) runs on the same clock.
- **A claim about the screen must be true on the screen:** "There's no green on your screen" meant no green caption (the word GREEN
  stays white until the payoff, then one coloured GREEN), navy straps instead of teal, and a numpy check of the MP4's test frames
  (no pixel with G more than 12 over R and B). The cover may promise the green dot; the Short never draws it.

  bottom read as a lollipop stick), filling the frame at 1.9x, with characters standing on its floor: big faces read where the six
  wall sensors of eye-rub-stars would have been specks. The card holds its gap on a marked dot while the beam turns grey.

  2.20-2.30 (`T.bursts`). Moving the word onto its sound fixed it (content SNR min 10.3 dB). Check `qc_inband` flags with a short
  window before re-voicing.
- **A raised brow above about 0.6 vanishes into his hair** (as one-eyebrow found): a skeptical squint is one brow up 0.6 and the
  other down 0.95.
- **A rubber suction cup needs a lip, a shine and a stalk**, or a dark red disc on his eye reads as a black eye.
- Voice: one take per block, 467 characters, no retakes. Render about 250 s for 1,030 frames.

**head-shake-reading** (29 Oct 2026, the curiopulse-daily-short routine at 00:03 IST, 00:03-01:55; a TEST Short, the pill arm

right? Now gently shake your HEAD." (after The Impossible Claim, The Question, The Direct Address); answer "Sharp again? Your EARS
are steering your eyes." at 4.35 s; button "Still reading? Nod yes." (nodding while reading is the same test a new way) into the
hook; YouTube aNBQO2osfZ4, playlist Why Does Your Body Do That?, cover uploaded in Studio; Instagram (Business Suite, Instagram
only, cover set) + the Page (fb.mjs 2328255954632658) 2 Nov 21:00 IST. 2 QA rounds, 18/18, rebuild matched (mix byte-identical).
World: blind-spot's `bed.js` (we ARE his phone) + new `shake.js` (the turned head, the ear's driver, cords, arrows, stopwatch,
burst) + `ward.js` (1948 sepia ward, iron bed, clamp bars, the HOLD STILL page, `wdOldFilm`).
- **A head that turns "no" or nods "yes", drawn as a sphere** (`shake.js`): `window.drawHead` is wrapped (the original kept), and a
  rig state with `turn = {yaw, pitch, gaze, smear, cross, squash}` draws the face projected on a ball: the far ear goes behind the
  head, the near one over it, the nose on a bigger sphere swings further than the eyes, the hair cap is carried round inside the
  skull. Reusable for any shake/nod/turn test.
- **Pupils of a turned head: the eyeball sits straight behind the lid along the way the FACE points**, not along the skull's
  radius (radial eyeballs made him cross-eyed when looking at the camera). `hsPupil` keeps the iris inside the lid. gaze [0, 0] =
  at the camera (the reflex: the face swings, the pupils stay); gaze = the face's angles = carried with the head.
- **We are the phone, shaken:** the swing is the camera's `sx` with `post.blur` from its speed (0.022 x px/s; 0.045 turned frame
  0 to mush), and frame 1 sits near the end of a swing (sharp, displaced). His arm reaches to the phone: the hand is pinned to a
  screen point just past the frame's corner (`phoneArm` turns it back into rig units every frame), so the arm swings with the
  phone; it is drawn again over the duvet. Labels on a shaking frame go in `post.overlay` (after the blur) or they smear.
- **Shared rhythms:** the phone's and the head's swing frequency and phase are cues (`ph_f`, `ph_ph0`, `yaw_f`); make_timeline
  lists each fastest moment (`swings1`, `swings2`, `heads`, `nods`) and audio.py puts a top-band swish on each.
- **Round 1 scored retention 7:** a 1948 wide shot held 2.4 s with only the camera moving; he now lifts the book on "read" and his
  head wobbles on "his head" until the bars clamp it. The clamp bars first ran over his body and read as jail bars: they stop
  behind his shoulders.
- Research: the J.C. account (NEJM 1952;246:458, reprinted Br J Ophthalmol 1964, PMC505972) is a scanned PDF behind PMC's bot
  check; the bed-bars and pulse details are from MedLink's summaries (search results), the 8.6 ms latency from Collewijn & Smeets
  2000, the demonstration from Scientific American.
- Voice: one take per block plus one retake ("In a hundredth of a second." ended faint in the speech band; "In a HUNDREDTH of a
  second!" fixed it), 423 characters.

**octopus-hearts** (28 Oct 2026, the curiopulse-daily-short routine fired at 21:58 IST, 21:58-23:35; THE SIXTH CURIOUS SHORT of
the topic test, the no-pill arm (11:30 slot); short arm, 72 words -> 31.33 s; hook formula The Impossible Claim, said to "you":
"You have one heart. An octopus has... THREE." (after The Question, The Direct Address, The Direct Address); answer "Two of them
are just for its gills." at 3.97 s; button "Three hearts... and it still skips cardio." into the hook; YouTube ZGudFA0cQuM,
30 Oct 11:30 IST, playlist Strange Nature (cover.jpg also uploaded in Studio's thumbnail slot); Instagram (Business Suite,
Instagram only, cover uploaded) and the Page (fb.mjs, video 2155813338477122) 2 Nov 18:30 IST; two QA rounds, qa.py 18/18 both;
`build.sh` into a fresh work folder reproduced the MP4 byte for byte (same md5)):
- **Research changed the story:** the queue line said blue blood is why it needs three hearts; Live Science's octopus biologist
  (Kirt Onthank) calls that naive (octopus hemocyanin is as good as hemoglobin in cold sea water). The real reason is pressure:
  blood leaves the gills weak, so a third heart re-pumps it, the job our heart's two sides do. Read the expert source before
  writing the mechanism line; the queue's angle is only a lead.
- **Three new files for an underwater world (borrow them):** `reef.js` (a sunny reef four metres down: the water column by
  camera height, a rippling surface, light shafts, a far reef wall and a mid reef of coral heads/sea fans/sponges painted once
  at parallax 0.25/0.55 (`rfPara`), a fish school (`rfFish`), sand with Worley-noise caustics as two drifting pattern tiles (a
  whole number of tiles per loop in x AND y), seagrass, marine snow, kelp at parallax 1.3 (`reefFront`, `kelp: false` where it
  would hide the action), `rfBubbles`, the octopus's rock (`reefRock`: encrusting coral, an anemone, a starfish), a barnacled
  treadmill with a jogging crab in a sweatband (`reefTreadmill`, `s`, `flip`)); `octo.js` (a procedural octopus: eight arms as
  22-segment spines `a + curl*u + tip*ramp(u)^2 + wave`, suckers on the curl side, a mantle + face as one shape, horizontal-pupil
  eyes with moods calm/smug/proud/shock/lazy/happy/focus/dizzy, a siphon and a jet `ocJet`; poses sit/count/jet/lounge,
  `ocWalkPose` (two arms as legs, six tucked in curls), `ocReachArm`, `ocMix`; GLASS mode `xray: 1`: one outline, gills that
  squeeze with squeeze marks, two gill hearts and the big heart (`ocHeart`, `flat` = grey), vessels whose blood dots flow by a
  clock, `sag` hangs the vessel after the gills like a wet noodle, `punch` fires the blood to every arm tip, `octoHeartGlow` for
  the glow layer); `diver.js` (the rig snorkelling: `DVPAL` yellow rash vest + fins, a dive mask the eyes read through, a snorkel
  clipped to the strap, floating hair, a stethoscope from his ears to a chest piece in his right hand `dvScope`, bubbles and a
  gasp, `dvHeartWindow`: his heart as two halves TO LUNGS / TO BODY with tape).
- **`W(phrase, k, s)` counts k words AFTER the match, like `E`:** `W("gills", 2, i)` meant "the word two after 'gills'", so five
  cues sat a word or two late (the BODY HEART label, the heart that stops, the gill squeeze, the tape, the lazy hearts). Use
  k = 0 with `s =` the index from `find`; assert the cues' order in make_timeline.py.
- **A snorkel mouthpiece beside the mouth reads as a headset mic:** clip the tube to the mask strap.
- **A glow-layer copy must know the state:** the stopped (grey) heart still bloomed pink until `octoHeartGlow` skipped it.
- **Two walking arms that swing in opposite directions merge:** each leg keeps its side (`s * 0.34`) and steps in turn.
- **A gag prop at 60 px does not read:** the crab on the treadmill needed the camera to push in on the punchline (zoom 1.95).
- Voice: 72 words, 407 characters, every take first time (whisper exact); 13,680 left on account 2 at 23:40 (17,240 at the start: the other pipelines spend from it too; resets 1 Nov).

Instagram (Business Suite, Instagram only, cover uploaded) and the Page (fb.mjs, video 2631055497376571) 1 Nov 21:00 IST; two
QA rounds, 18/18 both; `build.sh` into a fresh work folder reproduced it exactly):
- **Three new worlds, built for the topic (borrow them):** `ns_camp.js` (a mountain campsite at dusk: the alpenglow peak as a
  plate painted at 1.5x for a whip up to it, ridges, pines, a meadow; soft out-of-focus copies of every plate for close-ups,
  `ncSoft`, cross-faded by zoom; his tent, a lantern, an enamel mug that steams, a jar labelled NEUTRON STAR, a gingham table with
  legs that can break into a V (`ncTable({brk})`) or lie in two halves (`ncTableWreck`), a teaspoon with a glowing heap
  (`ncSpoon`, `ncBlob`: bulge, wobble, light cracks), a crater with the handle sticking out (`ncCrater`), dust, a soot puff,
  fireflies; the blown-off summit as its own plate), `ns_space.js` (stars and a nebula, `nsStar`: a spinning neutron star with
  hot spots, dipole field loops and two lighthouse beams from a tilted magnetic axis, front/back beam ordering; `nsSun`:
  granulation tile, limb darkening, prominences, a cave-in `k` that turns it blue-white; `nsCity`: a night skyline painted once;
  `nsSpan`: a measuring line with a label), `ns_stadium.js` (an atom as an empty night stadium: rows of seats as a bowl from a
  per-row ellipse `sdRow`, floodlights, a striped pitch, a pea on the centre spot, a tumbleweed, the squeeze `q` that folds the
  bowl in, packed peas `sdPacked`). Space shots used jupiter-no-ground's `jphero.js` (the spacesuit hero) as it was.
- **`T.E("a b c")` is the END OF THE FIRST WORD** (word k = 0), not of the phrase: `E("right out")` gave the end of "right".
  Write `E(phrase, k)` with k = the last word's index. And `W("a mountain.")` matched the ANSWER's "a MOUNTAIN", not the payoff's:
  the music's silence span then ran from 4.7 s to 23.8 s and the payoff's label popped at the shot's start (qc_audio showed the
  music stem at -180 dB in six shots: read that table every mix). Use `W("mountain", s=find("weighs a mountain"))`.
- **Labels go in the shot's `overlay`** (drawn after the light and the bloom): "OUR WHOLE SUN" drawn into ctx on a bright Sun was
  washed to nothing by the glow layer; in `overlay` it is crisp. A label that repeats the caption is wasted: it became
  "≈ 1.4 × THE SUN" (the number the words leave out).
- **A prop the hero handles must be drawn in FRONT of him:** the crater and the spoon's handle were drawn before him, so his legs
  hid the handle he was heaving at and the gag did not read (round 1). Drawn after him, with two fists drawn again over the
  handle's top, it reads at once. Also: "hiding behind a tiny jar" did not read as hiding; hands over his ears with his eyes
  squeezed shut did.
- **Props at a natural size:** the first jar and mug were head-sized (116 px wide next to a 132 px head) and the close-up read as a
  toy shop; at 0.6x they read as a jar and a mug, and frame 0 tightened to zoom 2.8 (head ≈ 390 px).
- **A tablecloth to the grass hides what falls under the table:** the crater was invisible in the crash; a cloth that ends at the
  legs (hem 128 px under the top) shows it.
- **lib.js's `E` has no inQuad / outQuad / outSine / outBounce:** the render died on `E.outQuad is not a function`; the template's
  lib.js now has them.
- **Sound:** THOOM in the pause after MOUNTAIN (a sub drop, a body slam, the table's crack, soft-clipped) -16.5 dB; the BOOM in its
  own pause -14.4; the beams' "vwoom" twice a turn (all under 320 Hz plus air above 5.6 kHz: it runs under words); the bottom's
  THUMP was -27 (too quiet) until +5 dB.
- Voice: two hook and three button seeds auditioned (`voice.synth` into the scratchpad, installed by hand): the first hook's
  "weighs?" sat -31.7 dB in the speech band; seed 19 kept it. 384 + 213 characters; 17,703 left on account 2 (resets 1 Nov).

**ear-rumble** (28 Oct 2026, nightly /cp next, 00:02-01:10 IST; a TEST Short, the no-pill arm (11:30 slot); the queue's first
topic (moved up 24 Oct as the Vulcan-salute hit's kin); short arm, 74 words -> 31.20 s; hook formula The Direct Address, the
instruction itself, "Yawn. A big one. Your ears RUMBLE, right?" (after The Direct Address, The Comparison, The Impossible
Claim); answer "Congrats. You just flexed your EAR." at 4.63 s; button "Go on. Rumble twice for yes." into the hook; YouTube
6QOky2URd9k, 29 Oct 11:30 IST, playlist Why Does Your Body Do That? (Studio's vertical slot showed an automatic frame: cover.jpg
uploaded there); Instagram (Business Suite, Instagram only, cover uploaded) and the Page (fb.mjs, video 2820187861686358) 1 Nov
18:30 IST; two QA rounds, 18/18 both; the title is the searched question ("ears rumble when I yawn"), not the queue's "Why can
some people..."):
- **A test half the viewers fail goes second, not first.** Only up to half can rumble at will (55 % of 1,853 self-reported, an
  upper bound: Hoyle et al., JNER 2024), but a big yawn sets it off for most people. So the hook is the yawn (everyone gets the
  rumble by 3 s), and "Now do it WITHOUT the yawn." is the 8-13 s re-do; the ones who can't are the joke ("The rest... just
  pulled a weird face."), played by his friend in a camp chair.
- **The subject's body part stays clear in frame 1:** a yawn's arms-up stretch put his elbows (the shortSleeve cuffs) over his
  ears, the very thing the Short is about. The stretch goes out SIDEWAYS below the ears (wrists at rig (±236, hip-212)); his ears
  are drawn bigger behind the rig's (`erBigEars` in the layer's `pre`: campChar now passes `o.pre`); rings shake out of them.
  The stretch must drop fast (0.3 s): a slow drop passed through a T-pose with open hands.
- **A muscle no one can see, as a character first, then as anatomy:** the answer's window on his ear shows a red bean in a
  sweatband doing a curl (`erFlexMuscle`), the comedy; the mechanism (14 s) dives into ears-pop's section with the eardrum's own
  muscle (`erTT`, drawn in a bony canal ABOVE the eustachian tube; ear.js's tube muscle left out with `noTubeMuscle`, or two red
  muscles compete), tight ticks on the drum and a drumstick's TOK. The section framed at zoom 1.6 about (205, 50): wider showed
  the inner ear's purple loops as the biggest thing, closer cut the muscle off the right edge.
- **A line with no picture of its own gets a gag:** "Sound went muffled?" had nothing on screen until fluffy earmuffs popped onto
  his ears; the crisp under them is a soft "crnch" (the crunch low-passed at 900 Hz), and the knob turns the second, big CRUNCH
  down while it sounds (an envelope from 1 to 0.2 over 0.2 s, the word shrinking from 118 to 54 px).
- **A device for disabled people, shown with care:** the hero zipped into a mummy sleeping bag (only his face, "can barely
  move"), an ear camera in his ear, what it sees (the eardrum twitching), a tablet with a scanning keyboard (switch access) that
  types H, I on two rumbles; nobody with the illness is drawn.
- Two-shot of two characters across the fire: hero at x 470, friend at 850, camera (668, 1034) zoom 1.58: at 1.66-1.74 the
  friend's head touched the covered right edge (round 2's only change).
- Voice: 74 words, 383 + 59 + 22 characters (the half line split into its own take for the joke's pause; one retake when
  whisper heard "Ken" for "can"); 18,077 left on account 2 after. About 3.6 minutes a render; build.sh's rebuild matched.

Now... SWAP!" (after The Question, The Question, The Direct Address); answer "Tangled, right? Your brain mails every order...
to BOTH hands." at 4.17 s; button "Now try it... FASTER." into the hook; YouTube 27B-0yL0PkI, 28 Oct 23:30 IST, playlist Your
Brain Is Weird (cover.jpg uploaded in Studio: the API's thumbnail showed an automatic frame there); Instagram (Business Suite,

- **A real hand for a test done with the whole hand (new `pathands.js`):** the rig's hand is a ball. `phHand` draws a flat hand
  from the back in rig units (palm, four fingers, thumb, one outline: stroke all, then fill all), `sy` squashes it across for a

  patting forearm and hand lie over his head. The first pat hand pointed its fingers UP and read as a wave: the fingers lie
  back over the head (ang π - 0.16) and droop as the wrist lifts. The rubbing elbow belongs by his side, not out in the air.
- **Pats as a schedule of contacts:** make_timeline.py writes each shot's contact times (`hook_pats`, `tangle_pats`,
  `slow_pats`...), the picture bounces the hand between them (`phBounce`, `phAround`) and audio.py puts a thud on each; the
  hook's own schedule (`pat0` + k / `pat_hz`) holds at negative time, so the loop's last frames pat on the same beat.
- **A glass head with a room inside (new `mailroom.js`):** fill the head's shape opaque first, then fade the room in with

  times (`mrCycles`, `mrContents`). A shredder on a counter: its confetti heap goes BESIDE it and stays below its face (it
  buried it), and the close-up is centred on it, not the brain.
- **A toddler from the rig (new `kitchen.js`):** the hiker at s 0.82, his head drawn again 1.32x about the neck (`drawHead` in
  the layer's `post`), a curl and a bib; what is inside his head goes in a round window with a dotted pointer (`kcWindow`): a
  glass dome on a face that must stay visible (the juice in his face) has no room. A window on the right starts below
  y 480: at y 382 it met the watermark.
- **The rebuild proof can lose the pill:** `WORK=<elsewhere> src/build.sh` read publish.json beside the work folder, found

**sharks-older-than-trees** (27 Oct 2026, nightly /cp next, 00:00-01:20 IST; THE FOURTH CURIOUS SHORT of the topic test
`kind-2026-10`; no-pill arm (11:30 slot); short arm, 71 words -> 33.73 s; hook formula The Comparison, put to "you" as a
two-second guess: "Which do you think is older... sharks, or TREES?"; answer "Sharks. Trees are the new kids." at 4.07 s; button
"So when you hug a tree... you're hugging the new kid." into the hook; YouTube ax-W5RVgnJg, 28 Oct 11:30 IST, playlist Strange
Nature (Studio's own thumbnail saved); Instagram (Business Suite, Instagram only, cover uploaded) and the Page (fb.mjs, video
1783193542942287) 31 Oct 18:30 IST; two QA rounds; the render of record is build.sh's own, from a fresh work folder):
- **A world built for the topic, 2 new files (borrow them):** `shore.js` (one stretch of coast from the land, the sea on the
  right with a low sun over it, drawn in four AGES from one state `S` that can morph: `SH_PALS` today / ordo / devo / meso and
  `shPal(a, b, k)`; `modern` (today's forest UN-GROWS toward its base, far trees first), `grass`, `rocks`, `moss` (cushions),
  `devo` (the first trees: `shFirstTree`, a bowed trunk with a diamond scar lattice and a crown of fronds that unroll from
  fiddleheads, `gt`/`gf`), far ridges and soft clouds sitting on the horizon at their own parallax (`shFar`), sun glitter
  (`shWaveLines`), foam that breathes along the coast; the big tree he hugs as `bigTrunk` + `bigCrown` with growth `g`),
  `creatures.js` (a cartoon shark `skShark`: flip, rot, look, blink, brows, smirk/grin with teeth, a waving pectoral fin,
  shades, and a black occluder in the glow layer; `skWaterFront`; `skSplash`, `skFin`; a sauropod `dnSauropod` with a walk
  cycle and a neck that bends down nose to nose; `dnFootprint`; `skFireball`; `tallyRock`; the NEW KID sticker `newKid`; the
  YEARS AGO counter `yearsHud` (rewind / fast-forward); `shTag`).
- **A hug that reads from the front:** the trunk is drawn IN FRONT of him in his layer's `post`, his body behind it, then his
  forearms, hands and HEAD drawn again over the trunk (`heroDraw`): his cheek on the bark, his arms round it.
- **Water in front of something that rises out of it is an IMAGE, not a fill** (round 1): a see-through patch, or a fill
  inside `clip()`, is left out of the shape map, so the light stage still saw the shark under the surface and drew its rim and
  shadow through the water. Painted into a canvas of its own and laid on with `drawImage`, it is one solid, flat, unlit thing
  that hides what is under it; in the sea's own gradient it shows no edge. Keep it clipped to the water and off the shore (`x0`).
- **Glow-layer coordinates after `screenSpace()`:** gctx is already at half scale there, so `softDot(gctx, x / 2, ...)` put the
  sun's bloom at half its position (a bright blob on the trunk). Use the same coordinates as on ctx. Under `applyCam` gctx has the
  camera too: draw glints at world coordinates.
- **Cut the hero's layer out of the glow layer** (`destination-out` of `layerC`) when glints or a sun sit behind him: the
  bloom otherwise shines through his body (here, through the trunk on his layer).
- **The faint goes onto the land:** a topple about his feet to the side where the tree was went into the sea and out of frame;
  he faints backward, onto the rock, and the camera follows.
- **An eye-roll and a brow waggle are the cheapest comedy a side-on cartoon animal has** (round 2, retention 7 -> 8): the shark
  squints on "older", waggles its brows with a grin on "sharks,", rolls its eyes up on "TREES?". A raised fin "me!" read as
  touching his hand: dropped.
- Render ~0.24 s a frame (~4.5 min for 1,012 frames). Studio's edit page opened fine this time.

**jupiter-no-ground** (26 Oct 2026, the 02:00 second Short, 02:04-03:45 IST; THE THIRD CURIOUS SHORT of the topic test
`kind-2026-10`; pill arm (23:30 slot); short arm, 68 words -> 34.43 s; hook formula The Impossible Claim, said to "you": "If you
fell into Jupiter, you'd never hit the GROUND." (the title, word for word); answer "Jupiter has no floor. Just sky... and more
sky." at 4.30 s; button "Still want to jump?" into the hook; YouTube lVK_uraLavc, 27 Oct 23:30 IST, playlist Strange Nature;
Instagram (Business Suite, Instagram only, cover uploaded) and the Page (fb.mjs, video 1763128764800387) 30 Oct 21:00 IST; two QA rounds; the render of record is build.sh's own):
- **A world built for the topic, in 3 new files (borrow them):** `jupiter.js` (Jupiter painted once pixel by pixel,
  `jpPaintDisc`: eighteen belts/zones from a table `JP_BANDS` whose edges ripple with value-noise turbulence stretched
  east-west, festoons, the Great Red Spot `JP_GRS` with swirls and a collar, white ovals, a lit side, terminator, limb
  darkening and a bluish limb haze; 2,400 px in ~1 s of init; `jpPlanet(cx, cy, R, {soft})` draws it at any size with a
  black occluder in the glow layer; `jpSpaceBg` stars + Sun; and THE FALL: `jpDeckWorld(cam, t, {mid, holes})`, three
  cloud decks (`DECKS`: ammonia, ammonium hydrosulfide, water; top/bottom altitude, colours, wind) drawn in perspective
  (`x = 540 + F (X - cx)/Z`, `y = yH + F (camY - Y)/Z`) as fields of ~1,100 puffs on a jittered grid whose cell grows with
  Z, sprites pre-fogged at 7 depths, a far sea to the horizon, zonal winds sliding each deck its own way, cut open at
  `JP_CUT` (a wall of puffs on the cut face, drawn in front of the hero: he sinks through a deck and the camera never goes
  into it), `jpHole` (his spread-eagle silhouette glowing in a deck's underside, with a dim shaft), `jpSkyDecor` (cirrus,
  a Sun, cumulonimbus towers with anvils, pre-hazed sprites); the medium `jpMedBg(m)` (0 gas, 1 soup, 2 syrup, 3 a hot
  dark ocean glowing from below with silvery ripples) and its 3D drifting bits), `jpprops.js` (NASA's Galileo probe under a
  striped parachute with `crush`, a brilliant-cut diamond `jpGem` + `jpGlint`, noodles, carrots, peas, pasta letters,
  pancakes, bubbles, the gauge AIR/SOUP/SYRUP/OCEAN `jpGauge`, `jpPill`, `jpBurst`, gradient speed lines, `jpFlash`
  lightning, `jpManPath` his silhouette), `jphero.js` (the rig in a blue spacesuit `JPPAL` with venus-day's helmet as
  `jpHelmet`: sweat, a noodle that drapes and slides off, heat glow, diamond eyes; a scarf tail that streams up when he
  falls fast and floats in the soup `jpScarfTail`; a selfie stick with the phone's back `jpSelfie`; a pinch of two fingers
  on the glass in front of his nose; poses dive / land / float / brace and `jpSwim(ph)`).
- **The camera falls WITH him; the hero stays put on screen** (FEET, HS, ZH = 6): `deckCam(yFeet, yH)` gives the camera's
  altitude from his, so his landing on a deck is exact (his feet at the deck's top at Z = 6). The fall is one key list
  (`fallY`) that both scenes.js and audio.py read (the wind's loudness follows the same keys).
- **The rule of three works as staging:** lands on deck 1 (shock), tries to land on deck 2 (hopeful), crosses his arms for
  deck 3 (unimpressed); POOF, POOF, a small pf. Each poof in its own pause.
- **A colour helper trap:** lib.js's `mixHex` returns `rgb(...)`, not a hex string; `rgba(mixHex(...), a)` parses it as hex and
  gives black (the far cloud sea came out as a black band). Use fx.js's `mixH` for anything that goes on to `rgba`.
- **A selfie reads when the phone's BACK is in the frame** (top left, on its stick, inside the key zone), not when the stick
  leaves the frame toward the camera.
- **A brown diagonal band across his cheek on a 420 px close-up** (frame 0): the drawn shadow (`form`) on the head along the
  raised arm behind it. venus-day's frontal key was not enough here; `form: 0.3` (with `toonAt 0.3`, `cast 0.4`) in the
  close-up's light made the face clean. Crop the face at full size on frame 0 and the cover.
- **Round 2 (retention 7 -> 8): a hypothesis can be SHOWN without a word.** 27-30.5 s ("Oh, and deeper down? Some scientists
  think it might rain...") had him floating in one pose; each lightning flash now sheds dark soot specks that fall and sparkle
  into tiny diamonds (`sootSpecks`), his eyes and head follow them, he drifts, and the push into the deep is 12 %.
- Studio's edit page said "Oops, something went wrong" again (as on 20, 25 and 26 Oct): the vertical thumbnail is unchecked (the
  API set cover.jpg); the channel switcher was not touched.
- Render time ~1 s a frame in the deck shots (two decks of ~1,100 sprites + walls); ~18 min for 1,033 frames.
- Voice: one take per block, 385 characters (39,963 left on account 2 before).

**one-finger** (26 Oct 2026, nightly /cp next, 00:00 IST; a TEST Short, the no-pill arm (11:30 slot); a VIEWER REQUEST from
@Neptune_8thPlan3t ("the perfect middle finger", promised in the channel's reply of 24 Oct), credited in the description and
the queued "Next up" comment; short arm, 67 words -> 33.17 s; hook formula The Question: "Make a fist. Can you raise ONLY your middle
finger?"; answer "Ring finger came too? They're... handcuffed." at 3.97 s; button "Now try the OTHER
hand... just don't point it at anyone." into the hook; YouTube I4272g9UqZE, 27 Oct 11:30 IST, playlist Why Does Your Body Do
That?; Instagram (Business Suite, Instagram only, cover uploaded) and the Page (fb.mjs, video 1132678476177994) 30 Oct 18:30 IST;
two QA rounds; byte-identical rebuild):
- **A fist as a 3D rig (`fist3d.js`, new):** hand-local centimetres (+y to the fingers, +x to the thumb, +z out of the palm),
  each finger three tapered capsules whose joints bend from a fist (`FK_FIST`) as `lift` goes 0 -> 1 (knuckle first, the
  others a little later), `creep` (a lift at the knuckle only), `squeeze`, `tremble`; the palm is the convex hull of a point
  cloud (`fkPalmPts`); every part is a depth-sorted item (painter's order), so the same rig shows a fist from the palm side,
  the side or the back; an orbiting camera (`fkCam`: yaw, pitch, distance, roll, `rotX` to turn the hand palm-down, `mirror`
  for the left hand; `fkKeys`). Handcuffs round two fingers (`fkCuffItems`: back half behind the bone, front half and lock box
  in front), the hand as rosy glass (`fkGlass`), the cords on the back of the hand with cuffs between them (`fkInside`,
  `fkCordCuffs`), a forearm for the over-the-shoulder view (`fkPovArm`: a 3D forearm pointing at the camera became a huge disc).
- **The raised middle finger, kept monetization-safe:** palm toward the camera (aimed at no one), on screen only for moments,
  a robin landing on its tip (`robin.js`: `ofBird`, perch, flap, moods, a whistle and a sweatband for the coach gag), and a
  close-up at "handcuffed" that puts the fingertip above the frame. The gesture's slang name is never said or written.
- **A creeping ring finger has to rise, not curl:** a knuckle-only creep pointed the ring finger's middle bone at the camera,
  so its own fingertip hid the cuff round its base and the handcuffs read as a grey "C". A half lift (all three joints,
  `liftR` 0.58) stands it up beside the middle finger: two fingers side by side, cuffed at the base, read at once (and
  "Ring finger came too?" reads better too). The cuffs need the camera square-on to the palm (yaw ~0): from the little-finger
  side the ring finger hid the middle finger's cuff.
- **A fist seen from the side reads as flat hook shapes** (yaw -0.9 .. -1.5 tried): the palm side (yaw ~-0.5) is the one
  that reads as a fist, and it is the viewer's own view of their fist.
- **A finger straight out from a palm-down hand looks like it points UP from the player's eyes:** the keyboard shot is from
  the little-finger side (yaw ~-2.0), where the stiff finger points sideways and the gadget's arm and cord read in profile.
- **A black piano on a dark sepia wall disappears:** mahogany, a warm rim along the lid and case top, a lamp glow behind.
- **`speech()`'s colour argument is the TEXT colour** (white text in a white bubble showed empty bubbles): `ofSpeech` has dark
  text and a tail toward the speaker.
- Worlds borrowed: sky-blue's `trail.js`, ring-finger's `parlour.js`; new `piano.js` (a keyboard and the gadget in 3D through
  fist3d's camera, a side-view grand piano whose lid slams, `PN_ROOM` = the parlour without its piano, the museum, marble
  busts that argue, a glass dome).
- **Put the instruction before the question when the test takes two steps** (round 1 -> 2): "Can you raise just one finger? Make a
  fist. Now only the MIDDLE one." put the finger up at 3.8 s and the answer at 4.96 s; "Make a fist. Can you raise ONLY your middle
  finger?" (seed 32 of three) put it up at 2.7 s and the answer at 3.97 s. A groove whose first beat sat on "Can" put that word at
  2.2 dB SNR: start it on the next word. A strong zoom blur on the punchline's click blurred the very picture of the joke: 0.025.
- Studio's video page answered "Oops, something went wrong" three times for the new upload (as on 20 and 25 Oct): the vertical
  thumbnail is unchecked (the API set cover.jpg); the channel switcher was not touched.
- Voice: 408 + 168 (button auditions) + 147 (hook auditions) characters; quota.mjs read 40,284 before. Renders about 4 minutes.
  The 48-hour check (the public grid, Instagram shortcodes decoded to times) found all four Reels of 24-25 Oct out on time.

short arm, 74 words -> 32.90 s; hook formula The Question: "Can you SHAKE your eyes? Try it, side to side."; answer "Can't? Your
eyes have a parking BRAKE." at 4.25 s; button "Still can't? Blame your parents." into the hook; YouTube 6Y0ZurlxUio, 26 Oct
23:30 IST, playlist Why Does Your Body Do That?; Instagram (Business Suite, Instagram only, cover uploaded) and the Page (fb.mjs,

- **Worlds (borrow them):** mosquito-bites' `camp.js` (the campsite at night) copied with `campFriend` taking `eyes` (big eyes
  under his glasses), `photo` ({k, at, drawAt}: a framed photo held up in both hands, in place of the mug), `noMug`, `headPost`,
  and `friendHat` moved in from mlab.js; `eyes.js`: `esEyes` (mirror-eyes' big eyes in head units, plus `cross`, a conjugate
  `buzz` that jumps every frame (`esBuzz`: a 15 Hz shake cannot be a sine at 30 fps, it aliases to zero) with `ghost` irises
  across its span, a `smear` for a hop, and a red brake caliper on each iris: `brake`, `brakeOpen`, `off` (flies off, spinning)),
  `esLamp` (the red (P) parking-brake lamp on a forehead), `esLever` (a car's handbrake lever: boot, ratchet quadrant, chrome rod,
  grip, red button; `ang` 0..1, slides in from the right), `esHummer` (a hovering hummingbird whose wings flip every frame),
  `esPhoto` (a framed family photo: 'shakers' (gran, mum, a kid, all buzzing, shake marks) or 'parents' (calipers and lamps)),

  BRAKE." ran over 2.7 s of his still, deadpan face. Round 2 gave every half second an action: a head shake on "Can't?", a look down
  at his own eyes on "Your eyes have a", a handbrake lever that slides in and ratchets up in four clicks on "parking" (he stares at
  it), the clamp and a punch-in on "BRAKE". The lever became one of the best gags: a metaphor in the answer line wants its object.

  before the punch.
- **A 1-in-12 figure reads as two rows of six faces with one lit**, not as a row of twelve (too small at 56 px each).
- **The friend's reveal needs a push-in:** at 2.35x his calipers were specks; the camera goes to 4.6x on "let go" so they read
  springing off, with a SPROING burst; the BZZZ waits for the sound in the pause after "go...".
- Voice: one take per block, 395 characters (40,284 left after). Three phrases aligned early by v3 were moved onto their speech-band
  bursts ("and try again", "in jumps", "in between"). Renders 4-7.5 minutes.

**lightning-strike / lightning-full** (27–28 Sep 2026, made before this skill):
- They established the visual language: the storm world, the hiker rig, x-ray anatomy, bloom, and per-word
  captions.
- The Kokoro TTS narration "sounded like someone was READING A BOOK". That is why every Short since is performed by
  Jessica on eleven_v3.

**hypnic-jerk** (29 Sep 2026):
- Split the hook into two takes ("hook" + "jump") so the jolt lands exactly. Comedy lives in the `gap` values
  ("Wow. Thanks, body." after 0.80 s).
- v3's alignment gives a pre-word breath to the word, so `snap_onsets()` moves starts to the waveform. Whisper
  timestamps are late; keep the ElevenLabs alignment.
- Narration ran 76 s. Per-block `tempo` (1.03–1.06 on explanations) plus pause tightening brought it to 70 s.
- Whisper heard "isn't instant" as "is an instant", so the line became "is actually a handover". Rephrase anything
  whisper mishears.
- Picture fixes:
  - Closed eyes rendered as brown discs; they are now a lash curve (blink ≥ 0.93).
  - The bloom washed out the silhouette in front of the moon; fixed with black occluders in `gctx`.
  - Split-screen captions go to `capY`.
- Mix: 53 words were under 10 dB. A slow voice leveler plus stronger ducking fixed it; this is now `mixlib`.
- AAC pushed a burst crack to −0.30 dBTP. Fixes: soften cracks, low-pass the SFX bus at 16 kHz, a −1.9 dBTP ceiling.

**finger-wrinkles** (29 Sep 2026):
- The cutaway works best with a camera that travels to each part as it is named (`sectionCam` / `camKeys`).
- Punchline SFX go after the punchline word; the music drops out for "Nope", "meh" and "Science".
- voice.py drops isolated mouth clicks at take edges; a 10 ms click at a take's end blocked trimming.
- The first custom cover: the RAISINS frame, set as the YouTube thumbnail. Business Suite's cover picker never
  loaded, which the API route (`cover_url`) fixes.
- qa.py calibration: 13/13; content-word SNR 14.1 dB (5 % under 6 dB); whisper WER 2.8 % (it dropped "How do we
  know?"; watch quick questions under busy music).

**The move to this skill** (29 Sep 2026):
- The factschannel repo became github.com/hmunna36/curiopulse (history kept).
- The local checkout is sparse, and the toolchain moved to `~/.cache/cp`.
- The engine became reusable:
  - `timeline_lib` / `sfxkit` / `mixlib` reproduce finger-wrinkles' timeline and mix bit for bit;
  - `fx.js` holds the shared scene helpers.

**brain-freeze** (30 Sep 2026, the first /cp Short, the nightly routine):
- New worlds: `diner.js` (neon diner, milkshake + bendy straw, `frostHead` icing his forehead, `heroAtCounter`) and
  `head.js` (side-view head cutaway with palate, vessels, artery, trigeminal nerve and tongue, driven by `sectionCam`).
  New sfxkit atoms: `ice_crack`, `slurp`.
- The dedicated cover (`SC.cover` rendered from a one-shot timeline copy) reads far better than any timeline frame.
- A hook whose strange moment lands late (6 s) needs foreshadowing from the first seconds: frost creeping in from the
  screen edges and onto his forehead while he still slurps.
- Every masked word in round 1 was a punchline SFX placed ON its word (title ice crack, gulper crack, squeeze
  creak/glide, shutter on "study", ticks on "thirty"). Put them in the pause after the word, from the start.
- qa.py fixes: contact-sheet labels ran ~0.6 s late (ffmpeg output `-r 2`), and whisper dropped the words that
  straddled its 30 s windows ("In one study" at 29.4 s). qa.py now samples every 15th frame and transcribes in
  <= 28 s pieces cut in the narration's pauses.
- Voice: 1,337 characters (3 blocks retaken or rewritten to get from 76.7 s to 73 s of narration).
- An audio-only fix doesn't need a re-render: remux the new mix.wav onto the rendered MP4 (`-c:v copy`).

  (chef/blunt with gleam), the hero chopping with `heroChopping`, tear streaks and tear-fountain arcs, gas wisps, the
  chef's hat and `PAL_CHEF`), `inside.js` (brick wall of cells, a cell cut open with a vacuole, molecules and pac-man
  enzymes), `eye.js` (macro eye with corneal nerves, tear gland, flood; brain icon with siren) and `lab.js` (a
  high-speed-camera rig with a guillotine, a ruler and droplet physics).
- New sfxkit atoms: `chop`, `chomp`, `gas_hiss`. Also fixed an engine bug: `riser()` could be one sample short
  (`glide` rounds), which crashed the broadcast.
- `charLayer`'s `post` callback runs in camera space, not rig space: to draw on the head (a hat), apply
  `translate(st.x, st.y); scale(st.s)` first. The chef hat was invisible for a whole render because of this.
- Research changed the script: the popular "chill the onion" tip is contested (the Cornell 2025 droplet team even
  suggests the fridge may not help), and the "40× more droplets" press figure isn't in the paper. Neither was used.
- The first voice pass ran 82.8 s. Free timing edits reached 78.9 s; cutting a whole block (the Ig Nobel line) got to
  72.4 s without a retake. Plan about 145 words, not 160, for a subscribe-line Short.
- qa.py's voice check fails on single quiet words ("Two" at 0.8 dB): a cut whoosh and a groove's first bar on a
  shot's first word. Start grooves after the first words of a shot, and keep gags (a washing machine) from running
  into the next line.
- A 10 s static end under the subscribe cue cost retention: push in slowly and keep something alive (the crowned
  onion's wiggle) while staying above y 1100.
- Claude in Chrome was connected at preflight but gone by ship time, about an hour later. Check it again right
  before step 16; the Reel is caught up next run.

**yawning-contagious** (2 Oct 2026, nightly /cp next; the first Short under the 40–50 s rule, 105 words → 49.9 s):
- New worlds: `bus.js` (night bus interior, passengers with their own simple rig so nobody looks like the hero,
  `heroOnBench`, the yawn wisp, the bus from outside), `yawnhead.js` (brain-freeze's head cutaway with a hinged jaw),
  `props.js` (cold pack, lab monitor, two-bar study chart, a dog that yawns, a book with lit words, a magnifying glass).
  The rig gained `FACES.yawn` (+ `jaw`, `squeeze`, `tear` on any face). New sfxkit atoms: `yawn_voice`,
  `stomach_growl`, `bus_hum`.
- Length: the first voice pass ran 54.7 s. The new `tighten=<s>` option (caps a block's pauses instead of cutting them
  to 0.30 s) plus small tempo/gap trims reached 49 s with no retake. Plan ~100 words for a 45–50 s Short.
- voice.py bug, fixed in the template: words after a take's SECOND pause cut were timed late (cut points were compared
  in already-shifted time); captions ran up to 0.4 s late on lines with two long pauses.
- Jessica can yawn a word: `and then... [yawning] yoooou.` (5 variants auditioned offline with `voice.synth`, chosen by
  pitch glide + whisper; `[yawns] YOU.` gave only a breath). 135 extra characters well spent.
- A caption colour typo (`"brain": P`, the palette dict) rendered the word black for a whole render; make_timeline
  now asserts every COLOR value is a hex string.
- 2D pointing at the camera failed twice (a ball; then pointing at his own chin). A magnifying glass with one giant
  suspicious eye read instantly. A free-floating sleeve = a third arm: move his own arm and draw props at `r.wrR`.
- First mix: 49 words under 10 dB (road bed 120–900 Hz, rattles, SFX tails ringing into the next line, the subscribe
  click landing on "if"). Beds under ~420 Hz + hits in the gaps → content words 17.4 dB clear.
- Three QA rounds; a static 6 s end shot got a slow push-in. Rebuild proven: identical mix and qa numbers.
- Business Suite's schedule date field is now a text box (D/M/YYYY): select all, type 4/10/2026, then click the day in
  the calendar that pops up. The thumbnail picker still never loads.

**stomach-growl** (3 Oct 2026, nightly /cp next; 94 words → 49.9 s):
- New worlds: `exam.js` (exam hall from the front: arched daylight windows with shafts and motes, wall clock with a
  ticking second hand, SILENCE sign, desks in depth, classmates `drawStudent` (bus passengers without legs, writing,
  turning to stare, glaring, shushing), the hero at a desk `heroAtDesk`/`heroDeskPose` (write, thumbs-up, whisper,
  shrug) in a maroon cardigan `EXAMPAL`, `growlRings`, `growlWord`), `gut.js` (x-ray torso with a plump stomach,
  duodenum and coils, a squeeze band with chevrons travelling the gut path `gutAt`; the tube in section with a moving
  pinch `tubeSection`/`tubeJuice`, bubbles, crumbs, worried bacteria; the tartan `bagpipe` stomach; a `vacuum`),
  `lab1912.js` (1912 lab, kymograph drum + tambour, x-ray window with a balloon, bow tie, `sepia()` + `oldFilm()`).
- New sfxkit atoms (template too): `scribble gulp balloon_inflate bagpipe_sound vacuum_whine piano rag key_click
  projector shush`. `svf_bp` needs an ARRAY of centre frequencies; a scalar crashes (`np.full(n, f)`).
- The 1912 shots rendered nearly black: `oldFilm`'s gate mask did `rect(); rrect(); fill('evenodd')`, but `rrect()`
  starts a new path, so it filled the whole frame at 85 %. Build a hole with sub-paths by hand. For sepia, a
  `color`-blend fill keeps the exposure; main.js's `grade()` tint is a multiply and darkens.
- A helper that returns early when `k <= 0` must still return what callers use (`bagpipe` returned undefined on the
  shot's first frame, lt = 0): the full render crashed at frame 690 after 3 minutes. Render each shot's first frame
  as a still before the full render.
- Hook: the big event (the growl) lands at 3.2 s, so frame 4 gets a small one (a gurgle ring, he freezes wide-eyed,
  a gurgle under 280 Hz below the whisper) and the opening frame keeps his face inside the key zone.
- Voice: the first pass ran 56.2 s; a whispered "dead-silent" dragged to 1.4 s. Three hook/loud variants auditioned
  offline (`voice.synth`, ~400 characters), three lines trimmed, gaps/tempos tightened → 49.1 s. qa.py's STOP list
  doesn't include "through": a quiet "through" under the groove scored −2.6 dB until the music dropped out for
  "squeezed through a tube" (the joke beat anyway).
- Business Suite: typing the caption opens a hashtag typeahead after "#reels"; a trailing space closes it. The
  thumbnail picker still never loads. The tool's output filter hides a SHA-256 hex string: compare it inside the
  page and return MATCH/MISMATCH.
- Two QA rounds (hook 7 → 8, squeeze visual 7 → 8). Rebuild proven: identical mix.wav and qa numbers.

**goosebumps** (3 Oct 2026, the first `/cp next youtube-only` second Short, the 06:00 routine; 104 words → 48.8 s):
- New worlds: `room.js` (living room at night, a couch in front of a TV that is the camera: `tvFlick`/`tvLight`; the
  hero seated with `couchScene`/`couchPose`, home outfit `HOMEPAL` with bare forearms, popcorn bucket and flying
  popcorn, `armBristles`, `headphones`, `musicNote`, the red button callout `fluffButton` with an alarm beacon), `arm.js`
  (macro forearm `macroArm` with per-hair `rise(u, j)`, `skinBump`, the plucked goose `drawGoose`, `lightSwitch`),
  `skin.js` (the skin in section: follicles each with an arrector pili muscle, a nerve with a branch to every muscle and
  a travelling signal, the surface bunching), `cat.js` (snowy yard, a ginger cat with `puff` 0..2 via `furRing`, a dog
  silhouette that snarls then gets scared). The rig gained `shortSleeve` in a palette (template too). New sfxkit atoms
  (template too): `honk meow cat_hiss purr growl whimper hair_zip shiver horror_stab alarm_bell`.
- Voice: one pass, 648 characters, 0 % WER on the first takes. 49.2 s of narration came down to 47.8 s with free edits
  only (tempo 1.05–1.07 on the explanations, gaps −0.05, `tighten` 0.28–0.40).
- **`master()` peak-normalises the music bus**: lowering the loudest element (the hook's drone) lifted every other
  section by 3–5 dB and broke the balance twice. Read `qc_audio.py`'s per-shot table after every score change
  (aim for music −31 … −35 dB per shot against a voice at −23) and move `levels={"music": …}`, not single gains.
- A word the take itself swallowed ("can", −38 dB, in "Even music can press it") failed the gate at 2.1 dB under the
  song in his headphones. Diegetic music starts small (an envelope, −9 → −5 dB) and opens on the beat after the line.
- A hook whose reveal lands late (the hairs rise at 4.5 s): frame 1 is the jump scare (TV flash, scream face, popcorn,
  hair on end, a horror stab) and the foreshadow is the same phenomenon, small: his arm hairs stand in the wide shot.
  No zoom blur and only a faint flash on frame 1: the first render's first frame was hazy.
- Additive glow on bright skin goes white. The "wave" glow sat on the hairs exactly as they rose and hid the reveal:
  keep glows on skin at ≤ 0.12 and put them just ahead of the action.
- Macro skin: a jittered grid of follicles reads as goose skin (random scatter read as a rash); uniform-width hairs
  read as nails (tapered polygons with a pore dot fixed it); the lit dome of a bump at alpha 0.6, its shadow at 0.24.
- Skin section: at 300 px spacing each muscle crossed the next follicle; 380 px cleared it. Check the diagonal reach of
  anything that leans.
- The render gate held this run twice (two Visual Algo renders): `wait-renders.sh 60 && …` chained in a background job
  works; write the README and notes meanwhile. Don't re-run audio.py while your own render is muxing mix.wav.
- The harness blocks `rm` with a glob after a `cd`: write stills into fresh folders instead of clearing old ones.
- The session was interrupted for 3 h after render 2. The lock went stale but nobody had taken it: `run-lock.sh acquire`
  again, re-check `yt.mjs upcoming`, the repo and the render gate, then carry on from `.work/`.
- Two QA rounds (sound 6 → 8.5, hook 7 → 8, look 7 → 8.5, safe area 7 → 9), then `build.sh` from the cached takes as
  the final render: `mix.wav` and `timeline.json` byte-identical, the same qa.py numbers (13/13).

**ears-pop** (4 Oct 2026, nightly /cp next; 108 words → 47.3 s):
- New worlds: `cabin.js` (a plane cabin at dusk: sunset windows whose horizon stays level while the cabin pitches with
  `rot`, runway streaks, a cloud deck, a `sun` glare; overhead bins and seatbelt signs; the hero in 12A via `cabinScene`
  / `cabPose` (grip the armrests, hands over ears, one ear, the big yawn, fists to the eyes); a travel pillow; a tray with
  `chipBag` (puff) and `waterBottle` (crush); passengers `drawPax`; the baby `drawBaby` (cry, wobble, smug, medal);
  `tearJets`, `screamArcs`, `earThrob`) and `ear.js` (the ear in section: canal, an eardrum that bows out or in, the air
  pocket with its three bones, cochlea, the eustachian tube to the back of the nose, the muscle that opens it, air as
  particles whose number is the pressure, `earFlow`, `padlock`, `headMap`). Helpers in its scenes.js: `secCam`,
  `pressureHud`, `popWord`, `popStar`, `speechR`, `loudWord`, `tickPill`.
- New sfxkit atoms (template too): `cork_pop burp cabin_bed chime baby_cry sneeze`.
- Voice: one pass, 643 characters, WER 0.9 % (whisper wrote "12" for "twelve"). Two alignment bugs, both from v3 tags:
  the take's trailing silence was given to its last word ("worse." ended 0.7 s after its take, into the next line), so
  voice.py now never lets a word end after its take (template too); and "[deadpan] Landing" started 0.27 s late in the
  alignment, so make_timeline moved it to the take's first loud frame (`first_loud(block start)`). Check the first word
  of every tagged take against the waveform (first loud frame vs the word's start).
- **Captions at y = 1470** (`capY` in every shot's return): the tray, the baby and the tube all sat in the band where
  captions normally go (1330). Still inside the key zone (it ends at 1640). The subscribe cue lifts them to 1150 as usual.
- **Measure the gags in the mix, not only the words.** Round 1 passed qa.py 13/13 with the voice 25 dB clear, but the
  burp sat at −30 dB, the padlock −31, the wail −26, the subscribe click −35 (RMS of mix.wav in each gag's window; the
  voice is about −16). After the fix they sit at −19…−27. A sound that carries a joke wants about −19…−24 dB in its gap.
- The music bus again: plucks at −22 dB were louder than the groove's kick (−30 at gain −14), so turning two drones down
  made the groove 4 dB louder. Set every music element relative to the groove first, then move `levels={"music": …}`.
- A long noisy sound (the wail) can run under a line if it is written as an envelope: loud only in the gaps between
  her words (cue to cue), 32 dB down under them, on the `bed` bus.
- Hands that go to the head (ears, eyes) must follow it: `cabPose` reaches for `rig(base).head + headDX/DY`, or the hand
  lands on his eye when the body leans.
- A frame-1 hook whose event lands at 3.3 s: open close enough to read his face (1.34×), and start the phenomenon small
  at once (his ears glow and ring from frame 2).
- The cover: comic bursts hide a face at thumbnail size; put them beside the head, not on it.
- Two QA rounds (sound 7 → 8.5, hook 7.5 → 8), then `build.sh` as the final render: `timeline.json`, `mix.wav` and the SRT
  byte-identical, the same qa.py numbers (the MP4's bytes differ between renders; its frames and numbers don't).
- **Business Suite at night:** the extension's Chrome window was minimized, so the page was `hidden`: the upload sat at
  0 %, screenshots and any `await` in the JavaScript tool timed out, while plain synchronous JavaScript still answered.
  Check `document.visibilityState` (and `window.screenY` / `outerWidth`: a minimized window reports the screen's height
  and about 471×226) right after opening Business Suite. What brought it back: `open -a "Google Chrome"`,
  `resize_window`, then `tabs_create_mcp` and an attempt to close the composer tab (it timed out, but the window came
  forward and the upload ran to 100 % at once). The video's "Add video" was clicked from JavaScript (find the button by
  its text and `.click()`), because ref clicks timed out.
- **The Reel's cover works now:** in the composer, Thumbnail → Upload image. Copy cover.jpg into the scratchpad, upload it
  into a collector input, arm the same click hook with it, click the "Upload image" tab and then the "Upload Image" link.

**sun-sneeze** (4 Oct 2026, the `/cp next youtube-only` second Short; 98 words → 45.7 s):
- New worlds: `street.js` (the street outside a cinema in late-afternoon sun: `streetBack` (far buildings, the plum wall in
  shade, posters, a dark foyer, the marquee with a neon CINEMA sign and chasing bulbs, a door leaf that swings out with
  `door` 0..1.12), `sunDraw` (the sun in screen space, glare `k`), `streetVeil` (the warm veil right of the marquee's
  shadow), `sunAt(x)`; the hero lit for daylight `sunChar` (a cool shade wash, a warm key from the right, `dark` for the
  foyer, a pavement shadow); the sneeze as acting: `sneezeAct(t, times)` → `sneezeFace` / `sneezePose` / `sneezeHead`,
  `sprayBurst`; popcorn `popBucket`, `kernel`, `popcornBurst` (kernels arc, land and stay); `shades` / `shadesShape`; the
  other cinema-goers `drawPed` (three builds; walk, turn, shield, wide eyes); the cold `germ`; `sparkle`) and
  `sneezehead.js` (his head in section, in profile: `headCut` with the eyeball, the eye's nerve and the nose's nerve
  (trigeminal, with its branch to the nasal lining) as Catmull-Rom cords that draw on and carry signals (`shNerve`,
  `shPulse`), a ring where they run close, a beam of light through the pupil, sparks across the gap, a shield at the
  nose, the brainstem, and the brain with a face, an arm and a big red SNEEZE button). In scenes.js: `achooWord`,
  `secLabel`, `guessStamp`, the framed family photo (`PAL_GRAN`, `PAL_KID`: the hero rig in other colours reads as his
  family), the doctor's chart whose letters type on and then fold into an acrostic.
- New sfxkit atom (template too): `tweet(seed, f)` (a sparrow, for daylight silences). `sneeze(seed, f0)` at 232 Hz is
  a gran, at 410 Hz a kid.
- **Daylight without losing the look:** the building is in shade (plum, dark), one hard warm wedge of sun is the glowing
  thing, and the hero is lit from that side (`sunChar`). Keep the sun's bloom low (gctx 0.42): the first stills were a
  white blob across a third of the frame.
- **Glow on a face hides it (again).** Every sneeze frame was a white flare: a nose sparkle whose envelope never ended
  (`tk` had a start and no end), a 0.35 flash on the character layer and a spray puff that started at his mouth. Give
  every effect an end, keep character flashes ≤ 0.15, start a spray below the chin.
- **`toScreen()` points need `screenSpace()` before you draw them.** After `applyCam`, `line()` and `softDot(gctx, …)` at a
  screen point landed 390 px away (on the marquee); helpers that set their own transform (`sparkle`, `bigWord`) hid the
  bug for a whole render.
- **A hook's "starts small at once" still needs its cause on screen.** The sneeze lands at 3.1 s; the nose prickle could
  not honestly start before the sunlight reached him, so he strides out faster (in the sun by 0.9 s) and the sparkle and
  twitch grow from 0.97 s. Round 1 scored the hook 7.5 with nothing strange before 1.75 s.
- **A stare needs his face big.** With the chart above him he was 190 px wide: the deadpan didn't land until
  `post.push` went 1.2× toward his face on the stare (the chart's rows stay in the zone, its header goes under the top
  band).
- Voice: 687 characters spent in all (the script in one pass, a new last line, seven audition takes). Whisper base.en cannot spell
  "achoo" ("a chew", "a true") or "Gesundheit" ("Gazoon tight"): the takes were right, so measure the word instead
  (length, level, pitch: the chosen "Ah-choo" is 1.1 s long). "Gesundheit" was dropped anyway for a callback everyone
  gets: "Bless you... again."
- **A first word can sit BEFORE its own sound.** "What helps?" (no tag): v3 gave "What" an 0.08 s slot where the take is
  still silent, and qa.py failed it at −4 dB against the room tone. Compare `first_loud(word start)` for any first word
  qa.py flags; move the word's window in make_timeline.py AFTER the shots, captions and cues are computed, so the picture
  and the mix don't move (timeline shots/captions/cues stayed identical).
- A sneeze is a punchline SFX: all eight sit in pauses (two after "and—", after "light.", three after "FIRE!", after
  "Subscribe!", inside "Bless you... again."). When a take leaves no pause ("It runs in families. And its…"), move the
  gag to the gap before the line (the cut on "FIRE!" lands on the family sneezing; the line then explains it).
- Two QA rounds (sound 6 → 8.5, hook 7.5 → 8, comedy 7.5 → 8), then `build.sh` as the final render.


**pins-needles** (5 Oct 2026, nightly /cp next; 105 words → 49.9 s):
- New worlds: `studio.js` (a meditation studio at dusk: round window with moon and bamboo, lanterns, mats, a bronze gong
  `drawGong` / `gongRings`; classmates in the lotus pose `drawYogi` (eyes closed / one eye / wide / glare, `shh`); the hero
  barefoot in a linen shirt `YOGA`, with a `drawShoe` override that draws bare feet with toes (`BAREFX.wig` wiggles them);
  `heroLayer` (charLayer with a whole-body rotation about a pivot, for falls) and `heroPt`; the lotus seat `crossLegs` +
  `POSE_LOTUS`; the leg's troubles: `legTint` (a blue tint inside the character layer), `legWash` (TV static), `kneeFace`
  (a tiny face on his knee: sleep / serene / shock), `drawPin` (pins and needles that jab in, or fade as dashed ghosts),
  `fizzStars`; the big side-view `bigFoot`), `nerve.js` (the folded leg as an x-ray in profile `legGeo(open)` / `xrayLeg`,
  an artery, the nerve with its tiny vessels `legNerve` (pinch, dead, flick, drain, refill), `legPulses`, `legSqueeze`,
  `legSparks`; the nerve's fibres firing by themselves `fibreBundle`, a needle `electrode`, a `scopePanel`) and `brainy.js`
  (the brain at its desk `brainGuy` with moods and arms, an old TV `tvSet` with four programmes: feed, NO SIGNAL, the map
  with a question mark, static).
- New sfxkit atoms (template too): `gong(dur, seed, f0)`, `singing_bowl(freq, dur)`, `tv_static(dur, seed)`. `qc_gags.py`
  (in the video's src) prints the RMS of every gag in its own window: copy it and edit its cue list.
- **An overlay drawn after the character ghosts whatever crosses it** (his hands went pale with the numb-leg wash, and the
  held leg stopped reading as a leg). Tint inside the character layer instead: `source-atop` in heroLayer's `post`.
- **Hook: when the strange thing is small in the wide shot, open ON it.** Round 1's frame 1 was a man getting up with a blue
  smudge on one leg (hook 7.5). Frame 1 is now a close-up of the sleeping knee (a face, Z z z) and the camera pulls out to
  the room in 0.95 s; qa.py's first-half-second motion went 8.2 → 43.7. Compute the pose before the camera to do this.
- A frontal rig can't cross its legs (the shins are too short): draw the lotus by hand from `post` with `noLegs`, then the
  forearms and hands again on top. A leg that must read as a leg lies out to the SIDE of the torso, never in front of it.
- **The music bus is normalised to its loudest element**: one choir pad at −17 pushed every groove down to −37 dB. Put a
  one-off pad at the grooves' own level (about −29) and move `levels={"music": …}`.
- A ding rings on into the next line: a 0.3 s bell plus the room put "You" at −5 dB. Under 0.2 s, or on the bed bus on the
  word it marks. The score stops dead for the reveal line ("You squashed a NERVE...") and returns for the next clause.
- v3 again gave four tagged words late or short ("[panicked] Pins", "[deadpan] badly", "[curious] needles?", "[whispers]
  quietly": the whisper got 0.09 s): `move_onset()` in its make_timeline.py moves them to the first loud frame, and the
  whispered last word runs to the end of its take. A whisper needs silence round it: no bed, no bell tail, the SHH before it.
- Voice: 898 characters (one pass ran 54.2 s; free edits reached 50.7 s; two lines trimmed and re-voiced → 48.6 s).
- **The render gate can be held for long stretches** (a Quiet Wins film render): queue the stills behind `wait-renders.sh`
  in a background job and write audio.py, the README and publish.json meanwhile.
- Business Suite with a hidden window (again): `open -a`, `resize_window` and a new tab did NOT bring it back this time,
  but the whole flow still worked without it: the upload ran to 100 %, ref clicks on page buttons did nothing, so buttons
  were clicked from JavaScript (find by text, `.click()`); coordinate clicks and typing worked on the Share step; hash the
  file with `.then()` into a window variable (no `await`). Only the thumbnail picker never loaded, so the Reel has an auto
  cover.
