  organ units: three loops of liquid round a sac, a bulge with a jelly flap on each loop, the snail dim, the nerve
  (`initVestib`, `vbDraw(t, {flow, lit, bend, drop, finish, glowA})`); beads riding the loops; a droplet with a face
  (`vbDrop`); a chequered finish line with a flag (`vbFinish`); a round window on his ear (`vbWindow(cam, C, R, S, k, t, o)`,
  `o.focus` to centre one loop); `vbOn(V, fn)`; the bulge up close in amp units (`ampDraw`: walls, beads, the ridge, five hair
  cells with faces (calm / alert / shout / strain / panic), their hairs into a jelly flap that bends (`bend`), nerve fibres
  with a signal); `dzBurst` (a word in a jagged burst)), `brain.js` (time-flies' brain as `dzBrain` with a 'dizzy' face
  (spiral eyes) and a 'smug' face; `dzStars` (stars round a head); `dzMegaphone`; a hair cell with a megaphone `dzCellGuy`;
  an eyeball with legs and a megaphone `dzEyeGuy`), `stage.js` (a ballet stage: curtains, a spotlight cone and pool, a
  floor (`stBack`, `stCurtains`, `stSpot`); the hero in a pink leotard and a tutu (`TUTUPAL`, `stTutu`); a pirouette as a
  paper-doll turn: his layer squeezed by cos of the turn in `pre` (the transform holds through the rig and `post`), his
  back drawn as hair over the face (`stHero`); a SPIN SIGNAL dial (`stDial`)). In its scenes: `hookA(tt)` / `hookCam(tt)`
  / `hookDraw` (valid at negative time: he pushes off), `spinW` / `ghostW` / `roomAng` (the room's turn, and the slower
  turn the other way his ears report after the stop), `nys(u)` (nystagmus: a slow drift and a snap back of the pupils),
  `organKeys`, `ampFlow` / `ampBend`, `stageTurn` (whole turns ending facing us), `buttonA`, `SC.loop`, `SC.cover`, and a
  light function that turns the back light round him with the room.
- **A spin reads when the room streams, not when he turns.** The camera rides the chair, so he faces us and the room
  slides behind him. A tall chair back hid the room and frame 0 read as a man sitting; cut to shoulder height, with
  swoosh arcs round him, speed lines and a 1.35 smear, it read as a spin at once.
- **A lamp passing behind a hero blooms through him**: the strip's lamps fade out within 420 px of the middle, and his
  head and torso are black in the glow layer (`ofOcclude`).
- **The loop at a fast spin:** the join (last frame to frame 0) differed by 12.7/255, less than an ordinary step at
  that speed (13–17): measure the join against the steps beside it, not against zero.
- **Business Suite's Reel composer changed (14 Oct 2026):** "Create reel" now shows "Share to" (not "Post to"),
  "Caption reel · optional", "Upload up to 3 videos" and a copyright check. Its upload never started while the
  extension's Chrome window was hidden: the page polled `rupload…/fb_video/<id>-0-1048576` (GET, HTTP 412) every ~9 s,
  sent no upload, and `requestAnimationFrame` never fired; `open -a "Google Chrome"`, `resize_window` and a new tab
  did not make it visible. The old composer had uploaded hidden. The caption typed with real keystrokes read back
  whole. `tabs_close_mcp` on the composer tab then hung twice (a leave-page prompt, presumably): leave such a tab, or

**ears-ring** (15 Oct 2026, nightly /cp next, fired at 06:30 IST; standard arm; 109 words → 47.37 s; hook formula: The Impossible Claim):
- New world files: `concert.js` (a rock gig's front row: the hall painted once and soft (`initConcert`, `cnBack`: truss, lamps
  whose beams sweep in the glow layer, haze, a drum kit and two players as shapes), `cnCrowd` (heads and horned hands that bob on
  the beat), `cnSpeaker` (a two-cone cabinet; `pump` pushes the cones), `cnBlast` (fat rings off a cone); the hero in a black band
  tee `CNPAL` (the rig's coat with the zip and the straps taken into the cloth, short sleeves) with a bolt print `cnTeeLogo`, arms
  as {e, w} `CN_ARM` (horns up, by his side, a finger in an ear, earplugs in), `cnHorns` (index and little finger up from a fist,
  the thumb across), `cnPlug` (orange foam earplugs), flying hair on a bang (`flyHair`), `cnHero(cam, t, A)`, `cnHeadPt`;
  `cnOcclude` (black in the glow layer where he stands, so beams behind him never shine through him); `ringLines` (the ring:
  wobbly bright arcs out of an ear)), `amp.js` (dizzy-spin's brain as `erBrain`; `erAmp(c, s, t, o)`: a scope, eight level lamps,
  a VOLUME knob scaled 0-10 with a red 11 past it (`erKnobAng`, `erKnobPt`, `ERK`), a grille; `noise` turns the scope to snow,
  `ring` to one thin line; `erAmpGlow`), `cells.js` (a row of five hair cells with faces on the floor of the snail, each with a
  staircase of six hairs that bend flat (`bend`), nerve fibres down to one cord with beads (`signal`), music waves with notes that
  slam down (`waves`), a dead cell that greys with X eyes and a halo (`gone`), a cell that pops up in a gap (`regrow`), a blue
  'bird' hue; `erTag` (a pill with a leader), `erMeter` (TO BRAIN with five bars), `erNote`), `room.js` (a silent room: acoustic
  foam as a grid of four-faced pyramids painted once, a wire floor, a SILENT ROOM sign that lights; yawning-contagious's
  passengers as `rmPassenger` with their faces as paint; him seated `rmHero`), `chicken.js` (`erChicken`: a white hen in
  profile with comb, wattle, a lid for deadpan or smug, sunglasses, a headbang about the neck; mirror her with scale(-1, 1)). In
  its scenes: `beatU` / `bangDown` (a headbang locked to the band's beat grid, cue `beat_p`, valid at negative time), `hookA` /
  `hookCam`, `gigDraw`, `brainWindow` (a round window over his head that shows the amp shot's own picture, `ampScene`, fitted
  inside it), `vKeys` (a screen-space camera from keys), `ampXform`, `yardBg`, `buttonA`, `SC.loop`, `SC.cover`. `qc_gags.py` is
  in its src.
- **A band that lands on frame 1:** the beat period is the time to the last chord divided by four (`STOP / 4`), frame 0 is a
  downbeat with his head at the bottom of a bang, and the band at the end plays on `DUR - k * beat`, so the loop is one more
  beat. The bang, the speaker's pump, the crowd's bob and the strobe all read `beatU(tt)`.
- **A loop shot draws its whole world on loop time.** The stage beams swept on clock time (`t`), so the last 0.4 s showed them
  where they are at 47 s, not at 0: the join measured 26.1/255 against an ordinary step of 17.2. Passing `tt = t - DUR` as the
  world's time made it 17.1. And `(t * k) % 1` is negative at negative time: an `arc` with a negative radius stopped the stills
  (`IndexSizeError`). Use `((x % 1) + 1) % 1`.
- **A glass head crowds the face; a window above it reads.** The brain and its amp inside his head (a glass dome) were too
  small next to his eyes, which then looked like two huge white discs when the camera pushed in. A round window over his head
  that shows the amp shot's own picture, fitted inside it, read at once, and the amp shot later is the same picture, large.
- **`crickets(t0, t1, bus, gain, ...)` takes a LINEAR gain.** Passed -30 it played the crickets at 30 times full scale; the sfx
  bus is scaled to its loudest peak, so every other effect came out about 25 dB too quiet (gags at -31...-34 dB). `db(-30)`
  fixed it (the template's sfxkit now reads a negative gain as dB). Low thumps set the bus peak too: this Short's `low_thud`
  takes 8 dB off whatever it is given.
- **The ring as a sound:** a sine at 6.3 kHz with a slow beat (a second sine 0.2 % higher) sits above the speech band, so it
  can run under the words at about -26 dB and still be heard in every pause; it climbs with the knob.
- **Two pending Reels get the same slot.** dizzy-spin's Reel was never scheduled (its slot 15 Oct 18:30 is not "busy"), so
  `yt.mjs upload` gave this Short's Reel 15 Oct 18:30 as well. Moved to 16 Oct 06:30 in publish.json by hand
  (publish.md, gotchas).
- Voice: the hook ran 5.2 s on its first take (the answer at 5.75 s); five auditions, and "You headbang at a concert, and now
  your ears are RINGING." (seed 5) ran 3.8 s: the answer at 4.43 s. The static line's seed 2 has "ring." 7 dB clearer in the
  speech band. The account's counter went 85,848 → 85,370.
- Two QA rounds, 19/19 each time; 5.4 minutes a render. Round 2: the loop on loop time, foam pyramids (the alternating wedges
  read as a maze), a narrower sign clear of the watermark, a puzzled "huh?" after the last chord instead of a half-lidded stare.

**two-noses** (17 Oct 2026, the second routine of the day: started 06:04 IST, YouTube scheduled by 07:00; short arm, 74 words →
34.17 s; hook formula: The Impossible Claim, "You've got two noses.", proved by the test, "Cross your fingers and rub your
nose in the gap." (the last three Shorts had all used The Direct Address); YouTube vW0AeAfgNe0, 17 Oct 23:30 IST, playlist
Your Brain Is Weird; Instagram queued by the API for 20 Oct 18:30 IST):
- **Aristotle's illusion, built on blocked-nostril's mirror world** (`mirror.js` borrowed as it was; `mirNose` takes
  `nostK` now: full nostrils plus a flat mouth read as a pig's snout once the hand is off his nose or the shot is wider).
  New `fingers.js`: his right hand at his nose in head units, back of the hand to us, thumb outside (`xHand`, `xHandPts`,
  `XF`): index and middle as tapered capsules with nails and creases, `cross` 0..1 (side by side .. crossed), `edges`
  (the two OUTSIDE edges glow), `tape` (the six-month volunteers), `lift` (up beside his cheek, for luck); `feltNose`
  (a see-through copy of his nose with a dashed yellow outline: the second nose he feels). `xHero` in scenes.js puts the
  arm where the hand needs it (the wrist from the hand's own points through `mirHeadLocal`). New `fingermap.js`: the
  brain's room in room space under a view (`fmView`), a screen YOUR FINGERS with the hand the brain believes in
  (`fmScreen`: cross, edges, marbles, noses, a stamp), `erBrain`, `fmBulb`, `fmZzz`.
- **The X has to read on frame 0.** With the knuckles side by side the index's lower half merged into the back of the
  hand (same colour, no edge to the light) and the hand read as two fingers pinching the nose. Knuckles spread (the
  crossing mid-finger), a thin outline on each finger (paint) and the index a shade darker fixed it.
- **A window drawn over the hero needs an opaque disc first, outside the clip.** Drawing inside `clip()` is left out
  of the light's shape map, so the map under the window still said "his head": the light shaded the window's picture
  with his head's outline (a visible arc across the brain room). One plain `fill` of the disc before clipping fixed it.
- **`T.W()` / `T.find()` ignore case and punctuation:** `W("TWO.")` found the claim's "two" at 0.48 s (the ghost nose
  showed for 20 s) and `W("Your fingers")` the hook's "your fingers". Give repeated words `s=` (the index of their line)
  and assert their order.
- **A short claim with a tag runs slow:** `[mischievously] You've got TWO noses.` took 2.3-3.0 s on four takes; the
  untagged "You've got two noses." took 1.4 s with the widest pitch range (164-374 Hz). That brought the answer from
  5.66 s to 4.75 s. Audition 3-5 wordings with `voice.synth` before settling a two-part hook.
- **Gags against the words, measured the house way:** mix RMS over the words ≈ −16 dB; the gags −18 to −23 dB in their
  gaps. The pause after "counts TWO." was too short for boing-boing (it ran into "Volunteers"): the weird block's gap
  went 0.44 → 0.72 s (free), the Short 33.9 → 34.2 s.
- Voice: 434 characters for the first takes + about 290 for auditions (claim ×5, payoff ×2); 80,472 before.
- Two QA rounds, every item 8+ in round 2 (round 1: retention 7.5, the viewer's test out of sight for 4 s in the brain's
  room; look 7.5, the snout and a too-small "caught on" window). About 5.4 minutes a render. Rebuild check:
  timeline.json, mix.wav and the MP4 byte-identical.
- **Facebook Page, second loop:** Business Suite (Post to: CurioPulse only, read back) took the blind-spot file
  (SHA-256 checked, tab visible, animation frames firing), but the upload bar ran 66 → 20 → 95 → 89 → 50 % and the
  only requests to rupload.facebook.com were OPTIONS pre-flights (200). Cancel → Discard Changes worked this time and the
  tab closed. Same as 17 Oct 01:00, so it is not the hidden-window stall: the POSTs never leave. `fb.mjs reel` (the Page
  by API) is the path once dominant-eye's API Reel is seen going out on 18 Oct 21:00. Pending: blind-spot (19 Oct 21:00)
  and two-noses (20 Oct 18:30), files in `~/Desktop/instagram-pending/`.

**roll-tongue** (18 Oct 2026, the nightly routine: started 00:03 IST, YouTube scheduled by 01:07; short arm, 74 words →
32.20 s; hook formula: The Question as the viewer's test ("Can you roll your tongue? Try it right now."), the answer a
Contrarian Flip ("Can't? Don't blame your parents.", 3.43 s); YouTube id7jJEKKNtI, 18 Oct 11:30 IST, playlist Why Does
Your Body Do That?; the first Short picked as the KIN OF A HIT: ring-finger had 16.2K views in 2.5 days, about six times
the middle, so the "can you?" tests went first and the foot-switch test moved up to next):

  hole at the tip once both curl: it reads as a tube even at thumbnail size), `flop` (a wide sad droop), `sway`,
  `xray` (dark see-through, `fib: {L, X, V}` lengthways / across / up-down fibres, `noBone` a dashed bone), `glowEdges`;
  `tongueSlice` (a slice going flat → U → tube; `bare` for a panel), `miniFace` (a little face with its tongue out, for

  with a gene bead running down, a ONE GENE stamp), `sciPhoto` (an old sepia print of a 1940s scientist who blushes,
  steams and hides behind a GENETICS book), `mailWindow` (an email typed letter by letter, SEND → SENT, it flies off).
- **The face's own mouth showed above the drawn tongue and read as a moustache.** Any mouth type drawFace does not know
  draws nothing: tongue-out faces use `mouth: 'none'`, and a shut face switches back to 'flat' when the tongue is in.
- **A U-shaped band with two glowing dots at its ends, in a round window, is a smiley face**, and over his face it was a
  creepy one. The slice went into a split-screen panel beside a small tongue with a dashed cut line, and its edges are
  lit along the band, not as dots.
- **His own failure next to the diagram's success carries the lesson**: under "both edges up at ONCE" the panel's slice
  rolls into a tube (TUBE!) while his real tongue's edges seesaw (`wobble(..., lag = π)`) and flop with a red cross.
- **Gags measured** (`qc_gags.py`, from eye-rub-stars, re-pointed): every one at −15…−25 dB in its gap.
- One QA round, every item 8+; the rebuild from the cached takes was byte-identical. About 3.9 minutes a render.

**foot-switch** (18 Oct 2026, the second Short of the day, 02:05–03:10 IST; the kin of the ring-finger hit):
- **The picture is the viewer's mirror** when the test has a side and a direction: his RIGHT hand and foot on the
  screen's right (the rig's 'R' side), so "clockwise" and the 6 read on screen exactly as the viewer does them. A 6
  drawn in the air in front of you is not mirrored in a mirror (only front-back flips), so the on-screen 6 is a plain 6.
- **A two-sentence instruction ran long**: "Circle your right foot clockwise. Now draw a six with your right hand." ran
  4.9–5.4 s on three takes (v3 gives the full stop its half second) and pushed the answer past 5 s; the same words as
  ONE sentence with "and" ran 4.4 s (seed 5), 4.1 s at tempo 1.06. Audition the hook before building on it.
- **A brain half drawn from the front reads as a yellow block when it is big**; time-flies' side-view brain (`tfBrain`,
  copied as `bossBrain` with a tie on its stem, wrapped in `actor`) read at once as "the boss". In the glass head the
  two halves stay front-view and small, pink, each with its own little face.
- **Two wheels on one belt** said "the brain wants them going the same way" without a word of science: the belt drawn
  light and IN FRONT of the wheels, its dashes moving; the HAND wheel cranked the 6's way drags the held FOOT wheel.
- New `foot.js`: `fsHero` (the office hero with his right leg raised toward us and the shoe seen toe-first circling
  round the ankle: `legUp`, `ph`, `shake`; arms by elbow and wrist from a fingertip target, `fsArmFor`), the 6 as a path
  (`sixTail`, `sixLoop`, `sixState`: the stroke, then the loop counter-clockwise; `sixTrail` draws what is done, with a
  comet), `footPhaseHook` (clockwise, a stutter, then the 6's way; valid at negative time), `footRing` (a fading ring
  with an arrowhead, any colour), `fsShoe` (laces, tread, `cat` ears / whiskers / a face, `meow`), the glass head
  (`glassHead`, `hemiPath`, `hemiFace`: two halves lit one by one), `cordPts` + `cord` (from a half of the brain,
  crossing in the neck, to a hand or a foot, beads running down), `towRope`, `fsBurst`. New `boss.js`: the brain's
  office wall, `bossBrain`, `bossWheel`, `bossBelt`, `wheelArrow`, `bossMonitor` (LIVE: YOUR FOOT).
- **`T.find` ignores punctuation**: `E("it.", s=...)` found the "it" of "fumbled it" and put a gag on "just"; search
  from the line's last phrase (`find("IMAGINING it.") + 1`) and assert the order.
- **Whisper heard "study" as "steady" in the whole-file transcript only**; the line played on its own transcribed right
  from the voice, the mix and the MP4. Check the isolated segment before moving sounds or re-voicing.
- **A gag's picture and its sound land together**: YOINK! first popped at "drags" while its slide whistle played after
  "along."; it moved to the pause.
- Two QA rounds, every item 8+; the from-scratch rebuild (`WORK=<fresh> src/build.sh`) was byte-identical. About 3.8
  minutes a render. The Studio thumbnail slot was grey: cover.jpg uploaded there ("Changes saved").
- **Meta, 18 Oct 03:04 IST: still "API access blocked" (code 200)** on both APIs; the Reel queued locally, files in
  `~/Desktop/instagram-pending/9-foot-switch-*`; Business Suite not tried (it looped at 00:15).

**hum-nose-pinched** (19 Oct 2026, the nightly routine: started 00:04 IST, YouTube scheduled by 01:00; short arm, 73 words →
30.83 s; hook formula: The Direct Address, the instruction itself, with the narrator humming along; answer "Gone? You just
locked your voice in a room with no door." at 4.68 s; YouTube 7A4VxK8d5Z8, 19 Oct 11:30 IST, playlist Why Does Your Body Do
That?; Instagram queued and the Page Reel scheduled for 21 Oct 21:00 IST):
- **The narrator can do the test with the viewer.** "Hum along with me... mmmmmmm... and pinch your nose." made Jessica hum
  for 1.1 s (a voiced glide 222 → 150 Hz) and the take still ran only 3.86 s; a wording with the hum as its own sentence
  ("Hum with me... Mmmmmm. Now pinch your nose.") hummed 2.2 s and ran 5.9 s. Whisper hears "Mmm" (WER 2.7 %), the caption
  shows MMMMMM (`DISPLAY`). Audition with `voice.synth` and measure the hum's voiced frames, not only the length.
- **His hum is the instrument, split by the pause mask** (`hum_span` in audio.py): a closed-mouth hum on the groove's
  tonic, whole in the narration's pauses and only under 250 Hz under her words; `dark` turns it dull and straining when the
  nose is pinched; a choke (mmMP!) in the gap after "nose.". The loop: the encore hum runs to the last sample, and frame 0's
  hum must start at the same level (round 1 had a 9 dB drop at the restart: −16.8 → −25.4; the first word comes 0.19 s in,
  and the pause mask's 0.12 s tail leaves only 0.07 s of full hum there).
- **A glass head in two passes** (`hum.js`): pass 1 under his hand (the glass, the face on it, the hidden caves), pass 2 over
  it (the throat, the voice box, the air beads, the arrows, the lips and a zipper), so the pinching hand never hides the
  airway. One outline round head + neck + puffed cheeks: stroke every subpath, then fill them all (round 1 drew an
  outline per circle). The glass is an opaque fill (lit like a thing, with a rim); everything inside it is paint.
- **A pinch that reads:** a fist below and to the right of the nose, the thumb up the right wing, the index finger
  diagonal under the nose to the left wing, both wings squeezed in (7 head units) with squeeze marks for 0.4 s. A V of two
  fingers from straight below read as a peace sign; a horizontal index under the nose read as a moustache.
- **The middle sinus (between the eyes) read as a third eye**: two big cheek caves only. The dust bunny (1.45×) asleep in
  the left cave, a rewind (VHS 0.45, ◀◀) to bring the fog back for "Breathing? Barely touches them.", and the encore
  blowing it out of his nose carried the weird fact and the button.
- **A const named like an fx.js function stalls the page with no [pageerror]**: `const toScreen` (fx.js has `function
  toScreen`) made render.js time out (`page.waitForFunction: Timeout 60000ms`). Check top-level names across every loaded
  script (`grep -oE "^(const|let|function) name"`).
- Voice: 383 characters for the takes + about 260 for auditions (three hooks, two answers); account 2 read 56,027 before the run and 55,788 after it.
- Two QA rounds: round 1 sound 7.5 (the loop's level drop) and the zipper hidden under the mouth drawing; round 2 every item
  8+. The final MP4 is the one build.sh made from the cached takes. About 4.3 minutes a render.
- **Meta, 19 Oct 00:05 IST: both APIs answer again** (`ig.mjs whoami`: 31 posts; `fb.mjs whoami`: the Page). `fb.mjs status`
  showed dominant-eye's Page Reel published 18 Oct 21:00 IST (the first API Page Reel seen going out) and the Page copies of
  blind-spot, two-noses, roll-tongue and foot-switch all scheduled (19 Oct 21:00, 20 Oct 18:30 and 21:00, 21 Oct 18:30).
  The 48-hour check on Instagram came from the public grid (instagram.com/curio_pulse_tv/reels/, read only): eye-rub stars
  and the chalkboard Reel (both due 18 Oct) on top. Business Suite was not opened this run (the session's permission guard
  refused it after a scratch script tried to read the encrypted token: never decrypt tokens outside ig.mjs).

**breathe-swallow** (19 Oct 2026, the second Short of the day: started 02:05 IST, YouTube scheduled by 02:51; short arm, 76 words →
32.70 s; hook formula: The Question as the viewer's test, "Can you swallow and breathe in at the same time? Try it."; answer "Can't,
right? Your spit just cut in line." at 3.85 s; YouTube pJIc92uQLiU, 19 Oct 23:30 IST, playlist Why Does Your Body Do That?;
Instagram queued and the Page Reel scheduled for 22 Oct 18:30 IST; the hit rule was already served: ring-finger's kin went out
as roll-tongue and foot-switch, so the queue's order stood):
- **A breath has no direction on a face seen from the front.** Cyan streaks curving into his nostrils read as whiskers on the
  stills; two little air puffs with faces flying in from the sides and BONKING off his nose (stars, an "oof" face, then flying
  away) read at once as "the breath can't get in". Keep them small (0.62 × his scale, smaller on a tighter camera) and let them
  stop beside the nose: at 1.6 s they sit on his cheeks for a beat, which is the most they should.
- **A hand on his own throat:** trail.js's trHero takes `armLew: {e, w}` (armAt with the elbow down by his side, the wrist under
  his chin); `IK` to the wrist put the elbow above his head (he looked like he was scratching it). The fingers lie along the side
  of the neck with their tips on a small bump (`bsThroat`) that rides up with each swallow; his chin goes up 16 units so the
  rig's short neck shows.
- New `throat.js`: his head and throat from the side as glass (hair, eye and ear so it is him), the air's road (nostril, over the
  roof of the mouth, down) and the spit's road (over the tongue, down) as dense polylines that light up and merge into ONE TUNNEL,
  then part into the windpipe (front, rings, the voice box with two little doors at its top) and the food pipe (behind); the
  soft palate that swings up; the lid (epiglottis) as a pink leaf with a face, hinged at the root of the tongue, standing up
  open and folded down shut; characters `swPuff` (calm / wait / hey / frozen / oof / happy), `swSpit` (grin / smug / whee),
  `swDrop` (sneak / oops); `swBrain` with a red PAUSE button; `swAt(road, u)`.
- **The window on his throat must grow with the push** (R × the camera's zoom factor), or the dive shows a giant blurred face
  with a small window at the bottom (round 1, 4.5 s).
- **The score can do the joke:** the music STOPS when the brain hits PAUSE (only a low dread under "and the lid slams"), the
  SLAM lands in the 0.38 s before "SHUT.", and the groove comes back with the air (WHOOSH).
- **qa.py failed a word the aligner had put 0.1 s early** ("Feel" at 8.42: its window held only the "f", 0.3 dB). Traced on
  narration.wav (the vowel 8.50-8.66, "your" 8.66-8.80, "throat" 8.86-9.07) and set in make_timeline.py with an assert; 18/18 after.
- **Turning the loud gags down 3 dB moved nothing** (the effects bus is normalised to its loudest sound); `levels={"sfx": -8.0}`
  (house -5) put them at -16…-22 dB against a voice at -16.2.
- Studio's thumbnail slot showed an automatic frame (the KOFF frame): cover.jpg uploaded there, Save, the slot and the sidebar
  preview then showed it.
- Two QA rounds; the final MP4 is build.sh's, from the cached takes (18/18, WER 0.0 %, SNR mean 19.5 dB). About 3.3 minutes a
  render.
- Voice: the hook and the answer were picked from five auditions (273 characters; the two winners installed as the takes), two
  more of the lid line (118, not used), 345 for the other takes; quota.mjs read 55,788 before and 55,463 after.
- **Meta by API, both first time:** `ig.mjs queue` (22 Oct 18:30, GitHub Actions) and `fb.mjs reel` (video 1121689396895408:
  `ready`, `scheduled` for 13:00 UTC). The 48-hour check had been done by the 00:00 run at 00:05 (nothing was due between).

**vulcan-salute** (20 Oct 2026, nightly /cp next, 00:00-01:40 IST; short arm, 75 words → 31.43 s; hook formula: The Direct
Address, the instruction itself, "Try the Vulcan salute: split your fingers, two and TWO."; answer "Can't? Your middle and ring
fingers are holding hands... under your skin." at 4.55 s; YouTube w5b_ru8URMQ, 20 Oct 11:30 IST, playlist Why Does Your Body Do
That?; Instagram queued and the Page Reel scheduled for 23 Oct 21:00 IST; the ring-finger hit's closest kin, by the queue's order):
- **FIRST: the cloud publisher missed a whole day.** GitHub Actions' scheduled runs of `ig-publish.yml` did not fire at all on
  19 Oct (on 18 Oct they ran 2.75-3.5 h late), so dominant-eye (19 Oct 18:30) and blind-spot (19 Oct 21:00) never went out on
  Instagram (the Page copies did: Facebook schedules natively). How it showed: `ig.mjs upcoming` still listed both as
  `queued` with "no cloud results yet", `ig.mjs whoami` said "publishing quota: 0 of 100 used in the last 24 h", and the
  public Actions API (`api.github.com/repos/hmunna36/curiopulse/actions/workflows/ig-publish.yml/runs`) had no run after 18 Oct
  17:33 UTC. Fix this run: both folders back with `git sparse-checkout add`, `ig.mjs queue <publish.json> --at` the next free
  slots (dominant-eye 22 Oct 21:00, blind-spot 23 Oct 18:30), and pushed AT ONCE, so a late cron run would not post the two
  overdue Reels back to back. Every later Reel moved a day; the user should decide on a backup trigger (the report says).
- **A raised hand, flat to the camera, in 2D** (`hand.js`): canonical = the back of the hand (little finger left, thumb right);
  the palm is the same drawing mirrored; turning it over is a squeeze through zero width with the details swapped at the edge.
  Finger angles carry the test: `split` (the salute: index+middle one way, ring+little the other), `wrong` (the little finger
  alone), `stuck` (the ring finger follows the middle: 3 + 1), `spread`, `tremble`, `lean`. Glass from the knuckles down shows the
  cords (orange middle, yellow ring), the strap between them as two little clasped hands, faces on the nails, X eyes (`deadR`),
  a sensor glove; `vsHelper` is the other hand gripping two fingers, and `room.js`'s `vsSt` puts that arm's wrist where the grip
  is (elbow low in front, wrist BELOW the grip, or the forearm crosses his mouth in a close-up).
- **Every subpath of a see-through union must turn the same way.** The palm outline went clockwise and the fingers
  anticlockwise: nonzero filling left holes where they overlap (skin showed through the glass at the knuckles).
- **Glass in rosy violet (#93507C with a light centre) reads as see-through skin; deep violet (#5E2F63) read as a rubber glove.**
- **`screenSpace()` already halves the glow layer:** a sparkle drawn into `gctx` with coordinates × 0.5 landed at half its
  position (top left). Only scale by hand after `gctx.setTransform(1, 0, 0, 1, 0, 0)`.
- **Panels below y 1000 stop at x 870:** the lab's ranking rows ran to x 936 and crossed into the buttons' column (the mask
  caught it); rows of 330 px centred at x 696, medals at +128.
- **A melody in the speech band cannot sit under dense narration:** a theremin (440-590 Hz) in the studio masked six words even
  ducked; dropped. `silence(mus, ...)` also silences `bwomp()` (it is on the music bus): end the groove before it instead.
- **A stamp that repeats the caption adds nothing:** DEAD LAST as a stamp sat under the DEAD LAST caption; an X-eyed ring finger
  and a little tombstone (RIP) on its row made the pun visual.
- Studio's video page answered "Oops, something went wrong" twice for the new upload (Chrome connected, direct URL), so the
  Studio thumbnail check is pending for the user (the API set cover.jpg; the vertical slot is unchecked). Possibly Studio is on
  another channel now (a new brand channel was made on 19 Oct); the channel switcher was not touched.
- Voice: 391 + 71 characters for the takes, 333 for auditions (three seeds each of the answer and the button, picked by how loud
  their last words are in the speech band: "skin." −7 dB, "you?" −3 dB against the take's median; two installed); quota.mjs read

- One QA round, every item 8+ (look 8: the studio is plain, the lab has no hero); the final MP4 is build.sh's (timeline.json and

**finger-sausage** (20 Oct 2026, the second Short of the day: started 02:04 IST, YouTube scheduled by 02:58; short arm, 74 words →
34.17 s; hook formula: The Direct Address, the instruction itself, "Touch your fingertips together near your eyes, and look PAST
them." (picked from 6 takes of 4 wordings: 3.96 s); answer "That floating sausage? Congrats... you are seeing double." at 4.25 s;
YouTube L08J-VnxDlg, 20 Oct 23:30 IST, playlist Your Brain Is Weird; Instagram queued and the Page Reel scheduled for 24 Oct
18:30 IST):
- **An illusion drawn the way it happens**: each eye's copy of his hands goes onto its own canvas (`fspov.js`: the left eye's
  shifted right by d/2, the right eye's left: crossed double images), both drawn at 46 % and their intersection
  (`destination-in`) drawn solid on top. With the tips' gap g smaller than d, the sausage appears by itself exactly where
  it should (the right eye's right fingertip to the left eye's left fingertip), with one-eye ghost gaps on either side.
  The ghost gaps belong to the LEFT eye on the left and the RIGHT eye on the right (round 1 had the tags reversed).
- **Put the illusion over something dark and plain**: over the passengers' bodies the doubled fingers read as a mushy
  band; moved up over the night windows (tip line at y 690 in pov space), the sausage reads at once. Hands that come up
  from the bottom corners read as "my hands"; hands coming in level from the sides read as a bar. Tint only the fingers'
  band (`fsTintCanvas(..., band)`): tinting the whole copies turned the passengers behind the ghost arms green.
- **Front view of fingertips touching**: under the chin, not across the face (a finger under the nose is a moustache,
  across the eyes it hides them); the eyes crossed and down at the tips, then up to the camera on "look PAST".
- **The brain's room as a machine**: two monitors (LEFT EYE / RIGHT EYE) hang on a rail; "stacks them anyway" is a STACK
  button and the monitors running together into one BOTH EYES screen (CLUNK), which aligns the backgrounds and doubles the
  fingers. A push into the stacked screen must clear the brain from under the captions: a fast, deep push (0.6×, 0.45 s).
- **A static stretch gets a beat**: the phone shot's last seconds now snap the doubled bus back to one picture on "shh"
  (the brain hushing it), with a handheld bob; the drawer gag has the brain cover the sausages with both arms, look at us,
  then shove it shut.
- **The bus world borrowed and lit**: yawning-contagious's `bus.js` with the passengers as `actor`s and their faces as
  `paint` (`passFace`, a sideways glance `side`, a lean away); its window glows scaled down (`glowA`) in close-ups so
  nothing blooms through his head.
- **`find()` ignores case and punctuation, again**: `iS = find("Look past them,")` found the hook's "look PAST them" and
  `E("eye...")` the "eye" of "Each eye" (the granny slid away 12 s early). Search from the line's own start and assert.
- **A caption that straddles a cut flashes**: "and look / PAST them." started 0.08 s before the cut to the POV (qa.py

  brain): two lines.

**circle-line** (23 Oct 2026, the second Short of the day: the 02:00 routine, 02:04-03:35 IST; short arm, 76 words -> 31.83 s; hook formula:
The Direct Address, the instruction itself, "Draw a circle with your left hand and a straight line with your right." (after Direct Address,
Statistic, Direct Address); answer "Bent, right? Your hands are copying each other's homework." at 4.29 s; YouTube XCPq2xvL02w, 24 Oct 11:30
IST, playlist Your Brain Is Weird; Instagram (Business Suite, Instagram only) and the Facebook Page (fb.mjs) 27 Oct 18:30 IST; the first
topic of the Queue, put there by the 00:00 run as the kin of the foot-switch hit):
- **A test that needs both hands:** the phone goes in the lap. The first line says which hand does which ("your left hand", "your right"),
  which also put "your" into the first eight words (the first wordings, "with one finger... the other", had none; qa.py warns).
- **Research check that changed a line:** the split-brain study usually quoted for this (Franz, Eliassen, Ivry & Gazzaniga 1996) did NOT
  test circles and lines: Experiment 1 was lines in the same or orthogonal directions (one patient), Experiment 2 three-sided rectangles,
  and the "50 figures" set (lines, curves, circles, squares) was judged better-drawn by two patients than by the controls on 63 % of
  trials. So the line says "drew both shapes... better than YOU", not "drew a perfect circle and line". The phantom-arm fact rests on
  Franz & Ramachandran 1998 (amputees who can move their phantom) and Osumi et al. 2015 (phantom circles made the real hand's vertical
  lines oval); only people who can MOVE the phantom show it, so the line keeps "circling a GHOST hand". Full texts: the Ivry lab PDF;
  Ramachandran & Hirstein's 1998 Brain review (rctn.org handout) describes the phantom experiment. Firecrawl was out of credits; WebSearch
  and pdftotext on WebFetch's saved PDF did the job.
- **Two trails that tell the story:** each fingertip leaves a glowing trail of its last cycle (`clTrail` in `clhands.js`: newest end
  brightest, a white comet at the tip), drawn from the paths themselves (`clTips(tt, b)`: b = how far the circle has leaked into the line);
  the line's bend is a function of time, so the trail history bends with it and the loop at negative time is a straight line again.
  A dashed straight guide beside the oval on "Bent," shows what he meant.
- **The brain's halves pulled apart with a bridge:** foot.js's halves (HEMI, hemiPath) moved out by 15 head units with a wooden footbridge
  between them, a plan on each half (a circle, a line that bends), chat bubbles, a ring that rolls over the bridge, scissors that cut it.
  **A face on a half: drop the middle folds.** The fold curves at y -8 and +8 crossed the mouths and every smile read as a frown; with a
  face only two short folds down the outer edge.
- **"Missing an arm" with the rig:** new `st.hideArm` ('L' | 'R', template character.js) skips one arm; the arm vanished in a puff and a
  ghost arm came back. A ghost drawn as a translucent white body bloomed into a blob; as one union outline it folded into a confusing
  shape; what read: each part (upper arm, forearm, hand) outlined and filled in turn with a dark see-through body, so the forearm sits in
  front of the upper arm (`clGhostArm`).
- **A dive into the head with the hands in a window:** at zoom 5 his drawing hands poke in at the frame's edges; they keep drawing during
  the 0.5 s dive and rest on the armrests (off frame) once inside, while a YOUR HANDS window shows the two drawings live; in the payoff
  the same window becomes 2 PATIENTS, 1996 with clean shapes and ticks.
- Mix: a held chord in the halves shot masked "gets" and "of" (music −27 dB against a voice at −22): down 11 dB. The music stops under
  "Two patients with that bridge cut". Gags −19.4 … −24.4 dB.
- Studio's thumbnail section already showed cover.jpg (the API's thumbnails.set took): nothing to upload (direct URL, no switcher).
- Voice: the hook in three wordings and three takes (pitch range by autocorrelation picked the liveliest), the payoff and the weird line
  three takes each by their last words; quota.mjs read 44,168 before and 43,699 after.
- Two QA rounds (round 1: look 7.5 for the frowning halves; the bend moved earlier to land by ~3 s); the final MP4 is build.sh's from a
  fresh work folder (byte-identical; 18/18, WER 0.0 %, voice mean 27.3 dB). About 4.4 minutes a render.

**tongue-nose** (24 Oct 2026, nightly /cp next, 00:00-01:25 IST; short arm, 72 words -> 32.17 s; hook formula: The Question, the
search question as the test, "Can you touch your nose with your tongue? Try it." (after Direct Address, Direct Address, Statistic);
answer "Close, right? Your tongue's on a LEASH." at 3.13 s; YouTube N1vgcsMvZjI, 24 Oct 23:30 IST, playlist Why Does Your Body Do That?;
Instagram (Business Suite, Instagram only) and the Facebook Page (fb.mjs) 27 Oct 21:00 IST):
- **A hit read on Instagram:** the Vulcan-salute Reel had 823,921 views by 24 Oct 00:05 (Business Suite → Published; the Reels round it
  1.6K-3.1K), so the tongue test (the same "can you make this part of you do it?" kind) was taken ahead of the Queue's first line.
  **Mind the teaser already promised:** circle-line's Reel caption says "the next one: why you can't see your own eyes move in a mirror",
  so that topic was kept straight after this one (one Reel late rather than three).
- **A tongue reaching UP, seen from the front (new `reach.js`, `tnTongueUp`):** the first version (tip under the nose, inside the open
  mouth) read as a pink blob in the mouth. What reads: the rig's jaw dropped (`face.jaw` 0.45 with the mouth open), the tongue WIDER than
  the mouth opening so it covers the upper lip, its underside (two blue veins, the fold, the strap to the floor of the mouth), a dark
  edge, darker where it leaves the lips and lighter at the tip (coming at us), a little summit flag on the nose tip as the target.
  Strain marks beside the mouth read as cat whiskers: a sweat drop running down the temple instead.
- **The side view as glass (new `side.js`, `sdSection`):** the head in profile in section units placed by `SDW`, the tongue as a middle
  line with a thickness profile (`sdTongueLine`, `sdAt` gives point, normal, half-thickness), the jaw and the throat bone, ties drawn as
  curves that straighten when `taut`, rings that squeeze it thinner and longer, a dashed bone struck out. Show the whole head first
  (zoom ~1.4), then travel to the strap, the throat bone, the tip. **Light the parts by where they anchor:** the fan from the inside of
  the chin is a FRONT tie, only the cords to the throat bone are BACK.
- **v3's alignment ran 0.6 s early** inside a block after a tightened pause ("It's tied down, front and back."): qc_inband read
  "tied" at -45 dB in its window; the speech-band envelope and whisper put the words later. make_timeline.py now re-times such words
  from the take's own bursts (`_bursts`: runs above -34 dB in 300-4000 Hz, 10 ms hops, gaps under 20 ms merged).
- **A cut inside a caption chunk cuts the caption short:** timeline_lib ends a chunk at a cut, so a cut 0.12 s after "IT'S TIED" made it
  flash and left 1.3 s uncaptioned (qa.py's "flash by" warning; round 1). Put a cut just before the chunk's first word.
- **The effects bus is peak-normalised, beds included:** a continuous strain rumble on the bed bus (x0.5) was the bus's peak and pushed
  every gag down to -27...-30 dB; at x0.06 they sat at -18...-25.
- **The glow layer in screen space already has the 0.5 scale** (`screenSpace()` sets it): pass full-resolution coordinates to
  `softDot(gctx, ...)`; halving them put the glows in the wrong place.
- **The crowd of ten (`tnMini`):** a miss and a reach look the same at 170 px unless the misses fall clearly short (reach 0.4-0.65) and
  a winner overlaps the nose with a small spark.
- Studio's vertical thumbnail slot showed an automatic frame (the side view): cover.jpg uploaded there, saved (direct URL, no switcher).
- Voice: one take per block, no retakes (419 characters; quota.mjs read 41,856 before). Research through WebSearch/WebFetch (Firecrawl
  not used); the 8-10 % / 50 % figures trace to Letourneau 2001 via Kaurani 2014 and BMJ 2014; De Felice 2001 (no strap in EDS) left out
  as disputed.
- Two QA rounds (round 1: retention 7.5 for the caption cut); the final MP4 is byte-identical to build.sh's from a fresh work folder
  (18/18, WER 0.0 %, voice mean 26.3 dB). About 3.8 minutes a render.

**mirror-eyes** (24 Oct 2026, the 02:00 second Short; 02:05-03:10 IST): "Look at your eyes in a mirror. Left, right. See them move?" /
"Nope. Your brain cuts out every jump, like a sneaky film editor." (4.83 s); hook formula The Direct Address.
- **The eyes, drawn again in head units (`eyes.js` `meEyes`)**, bigger than the rig's, over them: an iris, a pupil, lids, a squint, and
  a `smear` (a streak of irises between two looks). In the mirror the look JUMPS from one frame to the next (never travels): that is the
  test told in pictures; on the phone it SLIDES with streaks (the late camera). A dark socket ring plus a heavy outline read as GLASSES,
  and a lash flick at the outer corner as eyeliner: a thin outline and a lash line on the top lid only. mirror.js got `A.under` (drawn
  before the nose) and `A.noseK` (a smaller nose when the Short is not about the nose).
- **Inside his head as an editing room (`edRoom`, room space so it sits in the forehead window and then fills the frame):** a light table,
  a film strip of his own eyes, blind-spot's `erBrain` (`brain.js`) in a green visor with scissors in BOTH hands; the smeared frames
  cut out and dropped, the hole patched with copies of the next frame (1 1 1 2) to explain the stopped-clock illusion, and the window
  again in the button with the brain snipping three times a second (a callback that replaced loose snip badges round his face).
- **A prop that is the viewer's own test goes IN FRONT of him:** the wall clock drawn before the hero was set, so the lens's depth of
  field softened it and the pool light dimmed it; drawn after him (no overlap) it is sharp.
- **The sfx bus is peak-normalised, so a transient cannot be made louder by gain:** the SNIP stayed at -26 dB in its gap at any gain;
  soft-clipped (`tanh(3.2 x)`), the same peak read -19.9. Measure gag windows that start AFTER the word's tail (the left snip's
  window was the narrator's "so").
- **v3's alignment early again:** "See" (after "Left, right.") and "like" sat 0.1-0.15 s early: qa.py round 1 failed them at -16.7 and
  -3.6 dB. `_onto_burst()` in make_timeline.py moves a word onto the take's first speech-band burst after its aligned start.
- Business Suite: Instagram only (the Page untick by ref, read back), parts + SHA-256 in the page, cover via Upload image, Next ONCE by
  ref, then the Schedule option by ref (the footer then reads Schedule), 28 Oct 18:30 typed into the spinbuttons; "Reel scheduled ... 28
  Oct 2026"; Content -> Scheduled shows the Instagram row with the caption and the Page row from fb.mjs. The JS tool masked the
  collector's file names as "[BLOCKED: JWT token]": check sizes, not names. Studio's vertical thumbnail was an automatic frame (the
  phone): cover.jpg uploaded there and saved.
- The hook was re-voiced once (the first wording ran 5.0 s and put the answer at 5.55 s); 460 characters in all. Two QA rounds; the
  final MP4 is byte-identical to build.sh's. Queue refilled by a helper agent (10 sourced tests, 15 queued). About 3.8 minutes a render.

**lake-natron** (24 Oct 2026, a live session, 09:28-11:25 IST: the user asked "do 1 animated short now so that i will see it. it
should feel like a visual upgrade"; THE FIRST CURIOUS SHORT of the topic test `kind-2026-10`; short arm, 70 words -> 32.07 s; pill arm;
hook formula The Impossible Claim, said to "you": "You see that bird? This lake turned it to STONE."; answer "Not stone, actually...
it's a MUMMY." at 4.19 s; YouTube HYIP2vEE1Gc, 25 Oct 23:30 IST, playlist Strange Nature; Instagram (Business Suite, Instagram only,
cover uploaded) and the Page (fb.mjs, video 1439678644800221) 28 Oct 21:00 IST; title changed twice at the user's word: "mention lake
natron in title or comment" -> "Why Does Lake Natron Turn Animals to STONE?", then "but it does not turn into literal stone right?" /
"statue based alternative is good" -> "Why Does Lake Natron Turn Animals Into STATUES? 🦩", cover TURNED INTO / STATUES?!, caption
"...into statues. Not stone, though"):
- **A world built for the topic, in 4 new files (borrow them):** `natron.js` (a far plate painted once and drawn with parallax
  `NT.pFar`, sharp and a blurred copy for close-ups; the water as a 2.5D ground plane `ntPlane(cam, tilt)` / `ntAt(P, wx, u)`: u 0 =
  horizon at the plate's parallax, u 1 = the near line in world space, scale = zoom x u, x converging to a vanishing point; the
  plate mirrored in the water in 5 px rippled strips with a fresnel fade; soda-crust rafts, a glitter path, ripples, steaming springs,
  heat shimmer `ntShimmer`, warm motes, out-of-focus foreground crust; `ntLife`: wading groups, mid-lake snags with stone birds;
  `tilt` raises the horizon = a crane up), `canoe.js` (the hero seated in a canoe whose far rail is on his own layer `pre` and near
  side in `post`, so the boat is lit and sharp with him; `NATPAL` teal shirt; a pith helmet; a paddle that can crust; `tipR`: aim a
  pointing FINGERTIP at a world point by solving for the wrist; `dy`/`roll` bob the whole boat), `birds.js` (the lesser flamingo
  `nbFlamingo`: live or a chalk-white statue with crust; `nod` tips the beak DOWN; `nbBeakTip`, `nbNeckAt`; sprites for crowds
  `nbSmall` with reflections; `nbGroup`, `nbFlock`/`nbFlyer`; the swallow `nbSwallow` with `dry` and `crust` dials, `nbDrops`; dead
  branches `nbBranch`; `nbWaterOver`, `nbReflect`), `cast.js` (a mummy = the rig in `MUMPAL` with bandage lines and round
  sunglasses, a NATRON jar, a dust poof, a mud-cone nest, a fluff-ball chick, white salt islands on the plane, a spotted hyena).
- **The user watched the stills and steered while it was built:** "can we make the world more engaging?" (-> groups, snags,
  flocks across the sun, shimmer, motes, the boat's bob), "why is it red?" (salt-loving microbes; kept: it is the lake's real
  colour), "the flamingo head is awkward" (the beak was drawn pointing UP: a positive rotate turns a left-facing beak's tip
  upward; the eye was a big ring: now a deep bent beak with a black tip, a small golden eye, a blank pale one on the statue).
- **Never clip to hide what is under water:** a clipped shape is left out of the light's shape map, so the stage lit the wading
  flamingo as the canoe behind it and it looked see-through. Draw the whole thing, then a soft-edged patch of the water's own
  colour over the submerged part (`nbWaterOver`: a cached gradient image, unlit by design) and a faint mirrored copy (`nbReflect`).
- **Keep the sun off the subject:** with the sun behind the white statue's head, bloom ate it. The sun went up into the corner,
  its glow layer halved, the stage's `beams` 0.18 -> 0.08.
- **Hold no face before 12 s, even in the curious kind:** round 1 had him give us a flat look for 1.6 s on "Not stone,
  actually...". Round 2 gave the line an action that SHOWS the answer: he scratches the statue's neck, crust flakes fall, pink
  feathers show under it.
- **Fact framing:** the viral claim ("turns animals to stone") is not literal (Cerling: coated with sodium carbonate, dried, not
  calcified): the Short corrects itself in its answer line, and the title says STATUES.
- Render time: ~11 min (650-700 ms a frame in the wide shot with ~1,700 sprites + reflections); the final MP4 is byte-identical
  to build.sh's from a fresh work folder; qa.py 18/18 (voice mean 22.3 dB, WER 1.4 %). Gags measured in their gaps after
  round 2: knocks -23.8/-20.7, yank + eww -25.5, peep -23.2, honk -18.4, gurgle -18.0, yip -18.1 (narrator -16.7). Voice: one take
  per block (400 characters; quota.mjs read 41,137 before).

**venus-day** (25 Oct 2026, the nightly run, 13:22-18:10 IST (the routine fired late); THE SECOND CURIOUS SHORT of the topic test
`kind-2026-10`, promised by lake-natron's "Next up" comment; short arm, 68 words -> 31.73 s; no-pill arm (11:30 slot); hook formula
The Impossible Claim, said to "you": "On Venus, one day lasts longer than your whole YEAR."; answer "Venus is the laziest planet...
it barely spins." at 4.76 s; button "Happy birthday. It's still... Monday." into the hook; YouTube zdmZmB1AJBQ, 26 Oct 11:30 IST,
playlist Strange Nature; Instagram (Business Suite, Instagram only, cover uploaded) and the Page (fb.mjs, video 1394191682703946)
29 Oct 18:30 IST; two QA rounds; byte-identical rebuild):
- **Two worlds, built for the topic (borrow them):** `venus.js` (Venus's surface: a far plate under a lid of cloud with rolls, a soft
  brightening where the Sun is, broad shield volcanoes, one with a vent that breathes red and a plume, drawn with parallax; a 2.5D
  ground of basalt slabs (`vnPlane`, `vnAt`, `vnSlab`), dust, heat shimmer, motes, out-of-focus foreground slabs; a dawn that rises
  behind the hero (`dawn`, `dawnX`); props: `vnRock`, a desk calendar whose month flap flips like a split-flap and whose MONDAY page
  tears off onto another MONDAY (`vnCalendar`: `month`, `rip`), `vnBunting`, a cake with a "1" candle whose flame is a BULB
  (`vnCake`, `vnBulb`), `vnPopper`, a deck chair (`vnChairBack`/`vnChairFront`), `vnMug`; the hero in a silver heat suit `VNPAL`
  with a glass helmet that fogs when he blows (`vnHelmet` fog), a party hat on the helmet (`vnHat`), shades worn OVER the glass that
  flip up (`vnShades`), puffed cheeks, a cake held at the plate's rim by IK, a walk on the spot, and `rot`: the whole of him turned
  about his feet (a camera rotated about his feet, `camR`), for standing on a little planet) and `space.js` (stars and a galaxy
  band, the Sun with a corona, the dashed orbit and the lap so far as a gold arc, Venus from above its pole (a spiral of clouds round
  the polar vortex, a night side away from the Sun, a lit limb) that turns BACKWARDS while it goes round counterclockwise
  (`spOrbitAng`, `spPos`, `spHeroAng`: the real 224.7 / 243.0 days), the START line and the turn so far as a dial (`spStart`,
  `spDial`), the EARTH DAYS counter, label pills, and little Earth/Venus globes turning opposite ways (`spMiniGlobe`)).
- **qa.py now compares a spoken number as words:** whisper writes "two hundred and twenty-five" as "225"; with two numbers the
  transcript check failed at 14.3 % for words it heard right. `_num_words` (template too) expands a number of up to four digits.
- **Ragged brown patches on his cheeks (look 7 in round 1):** the cine look's drawn shadow ("form") has a ragged edge on a big head
  (300+ px) under a key light from almost straight above (`key [0.22, -0.86, 0.5]`), tinted brown by a warm `shadowTint`.
  `CP_LOOK=classic` was clean, `form: 0` was clean, `cast: 0` / `ao: 0` changed nothing. Fixed with a more frontal key
  (`[0.34, -0.6, 0.72]`), `toonAt: 0.3`, `form: 0.8`, `cast: 0.4`: a soft shade on the far cheek. Crop his face at full size in
  every close shot; the contact sheets are too small to show it.
- **Make the claim exact in the words:** "a day on Venus is longer than its year" is true for one TURN (243 days), not sunrise to
  sunrise (117 days, because the spin is backwards). The answer and the number lines say "to turn around ONCE"; the 117-day fact
  went into the pinned comment and the description (and invites comments) instead of a second idea in 31 s.
- **Gags measured in their gaps after the first mix:** the planet's creak -33 dB, the toot -28, the snore -27, the dawn "aah" -25
  (too quiet), the CLINK and the bwomp on the word "Oh". After: every gag -17.7 ... -25.2 dB (narrator -16.2); the CLINK moved to
  the pause after "Oh," (cue `oh_end`), and the pause before "Oh, and" grew 0.25 s (free) so the rip and the bwomp end before it.
  A music box playing the start of "Happy Birthday" (public domain) is a gag on the sfx bus that must end 0.1 s before "It's".
